# hired.video — Roadmap & Open Gaps

This file is the single home for **open + partial** work on hired.video: the
**Consolidated Gap Register**, the **TK1–TK11 parallel-agent isolation tracks**
that group it, and the **CxO go-live gap analysis**.

- Shipped / resolved / removed / won't-fix items live in **[DONE.md](DONE.md)**.
- Product, architecture & setup reference lives in **[README.md](README.md)**.

Gap IDs are globally unique across this file and DONE.md. When a row is
resolved, move it to DONE.md (don't delete it) and decrement its track's
"— N active" header count.

## Parallel Agent Isolation Tracks

Dispatch guide for running **multiple agents concurrently** without them touching the same files (no merge conflicts, no processing interference). The revenue tiers (T1-T4) in the Consolidated Gap Register below are for *prioritisation* and deliberately cut across files, so they are **not** safe isolation boundaries. These tracks (**TK1-TK11**, distinct from the revenue tiers) each own a **disjoint set of paths** plus a **reserved migration-number block**.

> **Run each track in its OWN git worktree + branch - this is what actually makes parallelism safe.** Disjoint paths are necessary but NOT sufficient: if you point two agents at the *same working copy*, even non-overlapping files can interleave mid-edit (a half-written file, a `tsc`/`vite`/test build racing against another agent's save, a shared dirty index). One worktree per track gives each agent an isolated checkout:
>
> ```bash
> git worktree add ../hired-<track> -b track/<track>   # e.g. ../hired-billing  -b track/billing
> ```
>
> Each agent works in its own `../hired-<track>` directory on its own branch, runs its own typecheck/build/tests, and you merge the branches back once each track's batch is green. Without this, the only safe degree of parallelism is one agent at a time.

**How to dispatch:** give each agent ONE track + its own worktree, then tell it: own track <TKn>, edit only files under its Owned paths, take the next migration number from its reserved block, pick work by filtering the Consolidated Gap Register for the track keywords, and update only your own rows. The keyword/path filter - not a frozen ID list - is the source of truth.

**Columns:** *Active* = open gaps left · *Done* = archived ✅ items classified to the track · **% Complete = Done / (Done + Active)** (how far along the track is, NOT a share-of-remaining). Recomputed 2026-06-20 (the re-tier pass re-classified every active **and** archived row with one keyword/path/anchor pass, so Active comes from the register's `### TKn` groups and Done from the same classifier over [DONE.md](DONE.md); reconciles exactly to the machine-counted 725 active / 579 done); all gap IDs globally unique.

**Where to find each track's gaps:** as of the 2026-06-20 re-tier pass the Consolidated Gap Register below is **physically grouped by these tracks** — every active gap lives under its `### TKn` heading, with its prior revenue tier preserved in the `Rev` column. To pick up a track, read its section in the register directly (no keyword filtering needed); the per-track counts in the table above are the authoritative active/done split.

### Shared / coordination-required files (NOT owned by any single track)

Touched by many tracks. An agent that must edit one should **append only** (never reorder/rewrite others' entries) and treat it as the merge point - these are also where worktree branches are most likely to conflict on merge:

- `api/src/app.ts` - route mounting (append your `app.route(...)`, do not reorder).
- `api/src/db/schema/index.ts` - schema barrel (append your export).
- `api/src/repositories/index.ts` - `buildRepos` DI factory (append your repo).
- `api/src/services/apiDeps.ts` - dep wiring (append).
- `shared/api.ts`, `shared/validation.ts`, `shared/projection.ts` - cross-cutting contracts (coordinate before changing a shared shape).
- `frontend/public/locales/*.json` - every track adds keys; stay inside your own namespace so JSON merges cleanly.
- **`README.md` -> Consolidated Gap Register** - the shared status ledger. Edit ONLY your track rows; append finished items to *Resolved - Done*. Re-pull / rebase before writing.

### Isolation caveats

- **Worktree per track is the hard requirement for true parallelism** (see callout above). Same working copy = serialise, even on disjoint files.
- **Migration numbering is the #1 cross-track collision hazard.** Two agents both grabbing the next number clash. The reserved blocks above prevent it; **TK1 platform-infra** owns the runner + global numbering and resolves cross-track FK ordering when a migration in one block depends on another.
- **Domain frontend pages belong to their domain track**, not TK11 frontend-ux-i18n (e.g. `Jobs.tsx` -> TK3 jobs-recruit). TK11 owns only shared chrome (AppShell/layout/ui/locales/SEO).
- Classification is heuristic (keyword/path) and applied to BOTH active + archived rows, so % Complete is an estimate (±a few rows per track). Before editing a file, an agent confirms the file sits under its Owned paths.
- **Gap IDs are globally unique** (de-dup pass renumbered colliding rows to #2101+; new gaps must take an id above the current max). Stale inbound `#NNNN` text references are tracked in #2188.

| 737 | T4 | 🔲 Open | Now | P1 | `bug` | **Drizzle-kit `_journal.json` is empty so there is no authoritative ledger of which migrations have been applied — drift like the `interview_*` tables sits silently until something queries them** (2026-05-17 — surfaced while closing #736) | [drizzle/meta/_journal.json](hired.video/api/drizzle/meta/_journal.json) is `{"entries":[]}`; migrations are applied manually via the `db:migrate` script (read one SQL file, split on `;`, run each statement). Nothing records what's been applied to which environment, so a fresh deploy or a partially-failed apply leaves the live DB out of sync with the schema and the failure surfaces only the next time a route happens to query the missing column/table. Fix: either (a) adopt a real `drizzle_migrations` ledger table + an apply-on-boot worker hook, or (b) add a `db:diff` CI check that fails the deploy when `schema/*.ts` declares a table missing from `information_schema.tables`. Both are larger than the 0089 patch we just shipped. Unblocks: the full set of silent schema-drift bugs that match #736's pattern. |

Fix: (a) port `handleMagicLink` into `AuthService.ts` alongside the other `handle*` handlers, with a deps-injected captcha port + email sender, mirroring the `handleLogin` test pattern; (b) add a `tests/contract/auth.contract.test.ts` that snapshots the frontend POST body shape for each auth send route against the backend's `safeParse` schema so a future "added a required field on the backend" silently passing on the frontend gets caught. Unblocks: silent-200 failure modes on the rescue path (and any future enumeration-safe handler) can't escape into production again. |

| 1588 | T4 | ✅ Done | Now | P1 | `bug` | **`GET /api/resumes/:id` and `GET /api/resumes/embedded/:id` exceed Cloudflare Worker CPU limit on slug-keyed lookups** (opened 2026-05-26 from live error log: `exceededCpu` on `/api/resumes/embedded/seanhogg` at 2026-05-26T21:16:29Z, request id `a01fcd1f5fdf80ba`, plus four prior `/api/resumes/seanhogg` failures within 25 s — same fingerprint `108bf7347bc9bb6792581caa33c576a5`). Shared handler is [handleGetResume](hired.video/api/src/services/resume/ResumeService.ts) — both routes converge there. Three suspected contributors, stacked: **(a) Missing `slug` index on `resume_information`** — [schema](hired.video/api/src/db/schema/resumes.ts) only declares `resume_information_user_id_idx` + `resume_information_parent_id_idx`; [findByIdOrSlug](hired.video/api/src/repositories/resumeRepository.ts) `eq(resumeInformation.slug, idOrSlug)` therefore seq-scans the whole table on every embed / detail load (DB-side, doesn't directly burn worker CPU but compounds with all the others by holding the request open). **(b) `normalizeContent` deep-walks the entire JSONB content on every read** — [normalizeJsonResume → remapKeys](hired.video/api/src/services/resume/parse.ts) re-allocates every object + array in the tree regardless of whether the row is already canonical lowercase-keyed. For rows where `content.metaData` carries embedded media descriptors (data URIs, large skillEvidence arrays from #1349 / [[project_skill_evidence]], or legacy AI-parse residuals), the walk is O(content-size) and dominates worker CPU. **(c) `c.json(result.body)` runs `JSON.stringify` over the full normalized blob** — content fields like `metaData`, `skillEvidence`, and the new `meta.template` bag can balloon to several MB on heavy users; even at canonical shape, the serialize step is unavoidable per-request. **Fix plan**: (1) **DB**: add `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS resume_information_slug_idx ON resume_information (slug) WHERE slug IS NOT NULL` as a new migration — partial-index since most rows have a UUID-derived slug, but the conditional keeps the index small. (2) **Read-path normalization**: short-circuit `normalizeContent` when the stored row is already canonical — detect by checking whether any top-level key starts with an uppercase character; if not, return the input plus default-array fills without walking. The PascalCase normalization is only meaningful for legacy hired.video JSON-Resume exports that haven't been re-saved since the migration — i.e. a vanishingly small share of read traffic. (3) **Payload diet**: audit what `content.metaData` and `content.skillEvidence` look like on the affected slug (`seanhogg`) — if they're carrying base64 media or 1000+ evidence rows, ship a one-time backfill that moves media references to the existing `metaData` media-attachment endpoint and dedupes skillEvidence. (4) **Diagnostic**: instrument [handleGetResume](hired.video/api/src/services/resume/ResumeService.ts) to emit `console.warn("[resumes/get] large content", { resumeId, contentSize, slug })` when stringified content exceeds 256 KB so we can tell whether (b) or (c) is the dominant cost. **Adjacent**: gap #979 already flags the 30 s worker CPU budget as fragile for any sync-fanout flow; gap #1570 documents the per-invocation subrequest cap. This one is the read-side analog. **Unblocks**: every embedded resume on every public profile + Chrome-extension embed + social-feed card — the embed has been silently 5XXing for every user whose content blob crossed the size threshold. **Partial 2026-06-14 — the two CPU-side fixes are in:** (1) **slug index** already shipped via [0141](hired.video/api/drizzle/0141_studio_projects_slug_column.sql) (`CREATE INDEX … resume_information_slug_idx … WHERE slug IS NOT NULL`); this pass also corrected the Drizzle schema declaration to the matching partial form so `db:push` won't try to recreate it ([resumes.ts](hired.video/api/src/db/schema/resumes.ts)). (2) **`normalizeContent` short-circuit** (see correction below). **CLOSED 2026-06-20:** the code-side CPU fixes are as complete as the codebase allows. (a) slug partial-index confirmed live (0141 + matching schema decl). (b) **moot for reads** — the prior note mis-cited a `content.ts`/`resume-normalize-content.test.ts` that never existed; verified the read path no longer deep-normalizes at all (`normalizeContent`/`handleGetResume`/`findByIdOrSlug`/`ResumeService.ts` were refactored away — `normalizeJsonResume` now has ZERO read-path callers, only the import/AI-parse path via `tryParseJsonResume`). The O(content) walk was nonetheless short-circuited at its true source ([parse.ts `normalizeJsonResume`](hired.video/api/src/services/resume/parse.ts) — canonical rows skip `remapKeys` via a cheap top-level uppercase-key scan, never mutating the caller's object; legacy PascalCase still remaps) + covered by [parse-normalize.test.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/resume/__tests__/parse-normalize.test.ts) (5 cases), so AI-parse/import of multi-MB content stops re-allocating the whole tree too. (c) `JSON.stringify` over the blob is irreducible per-request (acknowledged in the gap). The remaining items are NOT code fixes for this handler — the speculative one-time payload-diet backfill (base64 media / 1000+ skillEvidence rows) + the `>256 KB` diagnostic are data-ops/observability tracked under the worker-CPU umbrellas #1592/#979. |
| 1327 | T4 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P1 | `arch` | **Pattern 1 violation — 101 of 119 route files still call `getDb()` directly inside Hono handlers** (opened 2026-05-25 from the [Code Health Assessment](#code-health-assessment--2026-05-25)). Only ~24 surfaces (auth, resume, billing, companies, autofill, match, plan-override, settings) have been migrated to the `*-handlers.ts` + `*-deps.ts` pattern. The rest mix Hono adapter + business logic + Drizzle in one file, so they cannot be unit-tested without a live DB. Why: extraction work has not kept up with new-surface velocity. How to apply: pick the largest hot-path offender first — the next P0 candidates are `dashboard.ts`, `feed.ts`, `notifications.ts`, `jobs.ts` since they're called on every page load. | 1513 | T4 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `bug` | **CI install retry loop swallows persistent failures — `for i in 1 2 3; do npm install … && break; sleep 5; done` exits 0 even when all 3 attempts fail** (opened 2026-05-26 after the typecheck-against-empty-node_modules outage that motivated #1507's Tiptap install). The loop's overall exit code is the last `sleep` (always 0), so a hard install failure leaves node_modules unpopulated and silently lets the next step run with "Cannot find module 'react'" everywhere. Fix: track success in a variable and `exit 1` after the loop if none of the attempts succeeded, or chain three invocations via `||` so the overall command propagates the last non-zero exit. Applies to every install step in [.github/workflows/ci.yml](hired.video/.github/workflows/ci.yml). The 2026-05-26 fix swapping `npm ci` → `npm install` removes the lockfile-drift trigger but does not fix the latent bug. |

Backend nulls rejected by canonical schema"` — that's a live data-shape issue, not an environment problem. **Fix path**: (1) Audit the 14 failing files and add MSW (or `vi.stubGlobal('fetch', ...)`) handlers per file. | 511 | T1 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `feature` | **Gateway-internal retry billing semantics unspecified — risk of double-counting tokens against our user-side ledger** (2026-05-09) | The SDK response carries `_builderforce.retries: number` indicating how many times the gateway internally retried before returning a successful response. If the gateway charges the tenant key for each upstream attempt (typical pricing model), the `usage` we record from the final response captures all retries. We then debit that amount once on `tokenLedger.charge` — fine. BUT if the gateway returns 5xx after exhausting its internal retries AND we then advance to the NEXT profile in our cross-vendor chain (which the gateway doesn't know is a logically related call), the tenant has paid for both the failed retry burst AND the new profile's tokens. Our user-side ledger only sees the final successful charge. Result: tenant key spend > sum of user-side debits = gap. Fix: gateway documents whether `usage` on success includes retry tokens (yes/no), and whether failed-but-retried attempts are billed (yes/no). Builderforce SDK could expose `_builderforce.retryUsage[]` so our adapter can choose to debit each gateway retry separately. Until clarified: SA dashboard will under-report user-side spend vs tenant-key spend by the gateway-internal-retry delta. |

The non-TK6 domains in the title (recruiter, paid-media, career360, comics, graph, pipeline, profile, tremendous, location, moderation, sync, youtube) are still uncovered — keep open until those land. **CLOSED 2026-06-20:** every named domain now has genuine public-surface coverage — recruiter (`recruiter-schemas`/`agent-intake`), career360 (`outcomes`), comics (`scaffolder`), graph (`professionalGraph`), pipeline (`placement-and-bd-pipeline`), profile (`profile-service`), tremendous (`tremendous-client`), location (`geocoder-refresh`/`resolve-geo-slugs`), moderation (`contentScan`/`promptGuard`), sync (`connectors`/`extract-from-html`), youtube (`publishVideoUseCase`/`youtube`) all landed since the residual note. Today the Creator persona ([project_creator_persona_model](memory/project_creator_persona_model.md)) covers "personal user with a public channel" but has no surface for selling **time** — coaches, consultants, fractional executives, resume coaches, mentors, and creators offering 1:1 sessions have nowhere to (a) import a coaching CV as a credibility surface, (b) configure recurring availability + length-tiered session offerings (30 / 60 / 90 / 120 min, the four lengths the launch use case enumerated), (c) take payment via Stripe Connect, (d) accept reservations via magic link with public slot-grid showing only `available` / `booked` and no PII, (e) let bookers comment / reschedule / cancel without signup. **Strategic split** mirrors the contact-sourcing precedent ([docs/prd/integrations-and-sourcing.md](hired.video/docs/prd/integrations-and-sourcing.md), [project_contact_sourcing_burnrateos](memory/project_contact_sourcing_burnrateos.md)): BurnRateOS owns the **operator surface** (`/bookings` module — availability, services, Stripe Connect payouts, reservation pipeline, two-way calendar sync with Google + Outlook, comments queue, refund/dispute handling, ledger entries that flow into BurnRateOS accounting), exposes it via `api.burnrateos.com/v1/bookings/*` + an embeddable Booking Widget; SSO handoff if the user later opens the full BurnRateOS operator surface (multi-coach workspace, accounting reports, dispute centre). **Owner-only reservation visibility** is enforced at the API layer (not the consumer layer) — `GET /v1/bookings/hosts/:id/availability` returns slot status only for unauthenticated callers and full booker identity + payment status + comment count for host auth, reusing the single-seam pattern from #1173 / [project_globe_details_owner_only](memory/project_globe_details_owner_only.md). **Reference implementation**: net-new (grep across `api/src/services/`, `frontend/lib/services/`, and `shared/` returns zero matches for `availability` / `reservation` / `booking_*`). Full PRD at [docs/prd/bookings-and-reservations.md](hired.video/docs/prd/bookings-and-reservations.md) — covers data model (9 tables), provisioning bridge, API surface, calendar sync, Stripe Connect take-rate, 5-phase rollout. **Unblocks**: (a) Creator persona becomes monetizable beyond ad-supported content and token-pricing tiers; (b) opens the senior-exec fractional / advisory bookings track (#1627) without needing recruiter sourcing infrastructure; (c) gives BurnRateOS a high-value B2B-ops module that pairs with the contact-sourcing module to form a real operator OS; (d) is reused by every audience that wants 1:1 monetization — career coaches, resume coaches, mentors, technical consultants, fitness coaches, fractional CxOs. |

| 1220 | T4 | ✅ Done | Soon | P1 | `bug` | **CI deploy pipeline silently masks `db:push` failures, so SQL migrations apply onto a partially-bootstrapped DB and FK constraints to Drizzle-only tables fail** (opened 2026-05-25 — surfaced when [migration 0117](hired.video/api/drizzle/0117_recruiter_agent_followups.sql) deploy errored with `relation "recruiter_agent_runs" does not exist` despite the migration's defensive `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`). [.github/workflows/ci.yml](hired.video/.github/workflows/ci.yml) runs `db:push` (Drizzle schema sync) THEN `run-sql-migrations.mjs`. When `db:push` fails (e.g. [migration 0046](hired.video/api/drizzle/0046_sourcing_marketplace.sql) hit PostgreSQL's 1600-column limit on `job_items` because the out-of-tree legacy bootstrap left it bloated — see #1203), Drizzle-only tables like `recruiter_agent_runs`/`live_screening_sessions` never get created. SQL migrations then fail downstream with cryptic FK errors that look like the migration's fault. **Partial fix shipped 2026-05-25:** (i) [0117 hardened](hired.video/api/drizzle/0117_recruiter_agent_followups.sql) with a guarded DO-block FK addition so it survives this scenario; (ii) the four obsolete sourcing columns 0046 was adding to `job_items` (`accepts_sourcing_bids` / `sourcing_fee_cents` / `sourcing_deadline` / `sourcing_terms`) were dead — 0052 immediately drops them in favour of [`job_sourcing_listings`](hired.video/api/src/services/recruiter-marketplace/SourcingListingService.ts), and zero consumers remained across api/src/, shared/, or frontend/ — so they've been removed from 0046, taking the column-bloat blocker out of the deploy path. **Second fix shipped 2026-05-25** (after 0121's `archived_at` add on `job_items` hit the same 1600-column wall on the next deploy): (1) `run-sql-migrations.mjs` now tracks applied migrations in a runner-owned `_sql_migrations` table — each file runs at most once per database, eliminating the previous "replay every file every deploy" loop that made the `IF NOT EXISTS` defensive layer load-bearing; (2) [migration 0122](hired.video/api/drizzle/0122_reclaim_job_items_columns.sql) runs `VACUUM FULL "job_items"` to reclaim the dropped column slots — runs exactly once because the new runner records it as applied on success; (3) the failing `job_items.archived_at` add was extracted from 0121 into [migration 0123](hired.video/api/drizzle/0123_job_items_archived_at.sql) so it lands AFTER the slot reclamation; (4) [0121](hired.video/api/drizzle/0121_taxonomies_archive_versions.sql) had the broken `ALTER TABLE "job_items" ADD COLUMN "archived_at"` block excised with a header note pointing to 0122/0123. **Still open:** (a) make `db:push` failures fatal in CI by adding `set -e` and explicit exit-code propagation around the drizzle call (currently only the SQL-migrations step has `set -e`), (c) audit every Drizzle-only table in [KNOWN_MISSING_CREATE_TABLE](hired.video/api/scripts/migration-schema-parity.test.mjs) for downstream FK consumers and prepend defensive CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS in those consumer migrations the same way 0117 now does. Unblocks deploys against fresh / partial DBs without manual SQL intervention. **CLOSED 2026-06-20:** (a) done — the "Push DB schema" step in [ci.yml](hired.video/.github/workflows/ci.yml) is now an explicit `set -e` block (`cd api; npm run db:push`) with a header comment, so a `drizzle-kit push` failure is fatal and cannot be swallowed before the `if: always()` SQL-migrations step runs. (c) **superseded, deliberately NOT done** — prepending per-table defensive `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` to every consumer migration is exactly the per-instance-guard anti-pattern the standing rule forbids ("fix the mechanism, not each instance"); the mechanism WAS fixed by the second-fix `_sql_migrations` runner (each file runs at most once, so the defensive `IF NOT EXISTS` layer is no longer load-bearing) + the column-bloat root cause (0046/0121 excisions + 0122 VACUUM). With db:push now fail-fast, a genuine schema-sync failure stops the pipeline loudly instead of cascading into cryptic FK errors — the gap's actual goal. |

| 1662 | T3 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `feature` | **Job scraper has no staleness story — scraped rows stay `isActive=true` forever even after the source feed drops them** (2026-05-27, surfaced when reviewing the cron-driven scraper). The scraper ([api/src/services/job/scraper.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/job/scraper.ts)) inserts new `job_items` rows with `isActive: true` and dedupes by `sourceUrl` — but on re-encounter it just `skipped++` and moves on. Nothing tracks "last seen in feed at," nothing populates `expiresAt` from JSON-LD `validThrough` at insert, nothing pings `applyUrl` to detect 404 / "this job is closed" pages, and nothing flips `isActive=false` when a row stops appearing in its source feed. Net result: scraped jobs accumulate indefinitely and `/api/jobs` keeps surfacing closed listings. Fix (one bundled pass): (a) add `lastSeenAt` timestamp to `job_items` (or to a sidecar `job_items_external` table keyed by `(sourceId, sourceUrl)` so we don't bloat the main row), bumped via `UPDATE ... SET last_seen_at = now()` on re-encounter inside scraper.ts's "skipped" branch; (b) per-source TTL config in `jobBoardSources.config.staleAfterDays` (default 21); (c) daily sweep in scheduled.ts that flips `isActive=false WHERE lastSeenAt < now - ttl` (idempotent, cheap); (d) at insert time, populate `expiresAt` from JSON-LD `validThrough` when present (the JSON-LD extractor in `shared/scraping/jsonLd.ts` already parses it); (e) audit `/api/jobs` search filters to confirm `isActive=false` AND `expiresAt < now` are both excluded by default. Bundles cleanly with #473 (job_items.updatedAt doesn't auto-bump) — both touch the scraper's UPDATE path. Unblocks: search quality (no zombie listings), candidate trust (apply links actually work), gamification fairness (points for applying don't fire against dead jobs). |

| 2109 | T4 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `perf` | **Resume-tailor still re-emits the entire resume JSON + leans on a free-tier-only fallback chain — diff-only output contract + paid JSON primary** (split from #1801 fix 2026-05-30). With the schema guard (#1801) the 503 is gone, but the tailor call still asks the model to return the full `tailoredResume` document (`TailorResponseSchema.tailoredResume = ResumeContentSchema`), which (a) doubles prompt+completion tokens/latency/cost vs. returning only the edits, and (b) is why the schema was oversized in the first place. Highest-leverage follow-on: switch the contract to **diff-only** — model returns just `changes[]` (section/before/after/why), server applies edits to the stored `ResumeContent` and re-derives `tailoredResume` so the wire payload + the responseSchema both shrink dramatically; `validateTailorOutput`'s preserve-every-entry check becomes trivially satisfiable because untouched entries are never re-serialized. Touches [match-handlers.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/job/match-handlers.ts) (`buildTailorPrompt`, `TailorResponseSchema`/ingest, `handleTailor` persistence) + any frontend consumer that reads `changes`/`tailoredResume` ([ResumeTailor.tsx](hired.video/frontend/pages/tools/ResumeTailor.tsx)). Separately: the `match_tailor` profile chain ([providers.ts:223](hired.video/api/src/services/adapters/providers.ts#L223) + `FREE_TIER_FALLBACK_POOL`) is ~all free-tier models that themselves 429/timeout — a user-blocking foreground flow should have a paid JSON-capable primary (mirror `match`'s Cerebras primary) so the common case doesn't depend on the free pool being healthy. Unblocks: materially cheaper/faster tailoring + fewer transient retries. **UPDATE 2026-06-23 — profile half FIXED (root cause of "tailor never returns a result"):** the `match_tailor` profile had **no `maxTokens`** while it must emit the largest JSON of any use case — free/8B models truncated the full-resume echo to invalid JSON, and the paid terminal `anthropic/claude-opus-4-8` (which *requires* `max_tokens`) 400'd on the unset value, so the whole fallback chain could exhaust with no result. Profile now has a reliable JSON-capable paid primary (`anthropic/claude-opus-4-8`, temp 0.3) + explicit `maxTokens: 8000` (propagates to the appended fallback chain via `withFallbackChain`/`cloneForChain`), with a free `qwen3-next` first fallback for cost ([providers.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/adapters/providers.ts) `match_tailor`). **REMAINING:** the diff-only output contract (model returns only `changes[]`, server applies + re-derives `tailoredResume`) — still the right structural win to cut token/latency/cost in half and make `validateTailorOutput` trivially satisfiable. |

Until then the working builder stays (retiring it now would break screening creation). Unblocks: one authoring surface for screening + enforces the no-standalone-builders rule. (Possible enforcement follow-on: an ESLint rule flagging new `pages/**/*Builder.tsx` / wizard form pages.) |

How to apply: add `audit_touchpoints` polymorphic table (entityType varchar + entityId uuid + type varchar + payload jsonb + performedByUserId + createdAt + index on (entityType, entityId, createdAt)) under a new `audit.ts` schema; provide a shared `recordTouchpoint(entityType, entityId, type, payload)` service; migrate `pipelineTouchpoints` rows with a backfill; deprecate the per-aggregate copies. Should land before #1349 Phase 2 so timesheets ride the shared table on day one. |

| 2141 | T3 | 🔲 Open | Later | P3 | `feature` | **Predictive Assessments battery ships validated-INSPIRED instruments — not psychometrically normed scores** (opened 2026-05-25 alongside the [predictive-assessments tool](hired.video/frontend/pages/tools/PredictiveAssessments.tsx) launch). The Big Five uses the public-domain IPIP-NEO short form (10 items); the cognitive sample, SJT, EQ, and DISC are platform-derived heuristic batteries that score the right things but aren't calibrated against a representative norming sample. HireVue's 20+-year moat is exactly this calibration — scores you can defend in court for adverse-impact analysis. To close: (a) collect anonymous response data from the existing `assessment_runs` table, (b) compute platform-level percentile distributions per dimension per assessment, (c) update the scoring service to convert raw 0..1 scores into z-scores against the platform norms, (d) publish a methodology note + adverse-impact analysis so employers can defensibly use scores in screening. Until then, the on-page copy is explicit that scores are descriptive, not predictive — and the assessments are not surfaced to recruiters as a screening filter. Unblocks: positioning the assessment battery as a screening signal rather than a candidate self-discovery tool, full HireVue parity claim. |

| 400 | T4 | 🔲 Open | Now | P1 | `arch` | **Persona-axis architecture inconsistency across `/resumes`, `/jobs`, `/people`, `/companies` + dead `ownership === "tracked"` tab on `/resumes`** (2026-05-05) | Each entity page improvises its own role-aware tab structure with three different role-detection patterns (`usePermissions().isEmployer`, `createRolePermissions(user.role).canManageJobs`, inline `rolePermissions.isRecruiter`). `/resumes` is the only entity surface that hasn't been split into job-seeker (`resume_information`) vs hiring-side (`candidate_resumes`, migration 0027) data sources — `Resumes.tsx:1342` builds tabs against owned resumes for both personas, so an employer sees the empty job-seeker UI. Fix: extract a shared `EntityHubShell` that consumes a tab descriptor `{ key, label, icon, roleGate, dataSource, cardRenderer, emptyState }` per the studio-workspaces `canActFor()` precedent in `services/studio/workspace.ts`; collapse `/services/candidates` into `/resumes?tab=sourced`; delete the dead `ownership === "tracked"` filter and Candidates tab on `/resumes`; add `canSourceCandidates` to `RolePermissions` so every persona-axis check funnels through one predicate. Unblocks: `/resumes` actually serves recruiter/employer; master-resume / Upload / Create Resume concepts stop leaking outside job_seeker; the four entity pages share one persona shell so a future fifth page inherits the contract instead of re-improvising. Identified 2026-05-05 from user report — employer logs into `/resumes`, sees "No matching resumes" empty card despite there being a fully-built sourcing surface a redirect away. |

| 742 | T4 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P1 | `bug` | **Rendered MP4 doesn't match the editor's Preview output** (2026-05-17 — reported by sean@: "the VIDEO that is being generated also doesnt match the version showin in the preview") | **Architectural finding on closer inspection (2026-05-17):** the per-frame canvas render path is ALREADY unified — both [VideoPreview.tsx::drawFrame](hired.video/frontend/components/VideoPreview.tsx) (RAF) and [video-exporter.ts::WebCodecs loop](hired.video/frontend/lib/renderer/video-exporter.ts) call the same `render(ctx, compiledVideo, time)` from [frame-renderer.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/renderer/frame-renderer.ts). The only divergence flag is `{ previewMode: true }` which suppresses the first-scene watermark in preview only. So the drift the user reported is NOT scene-graph divergence; it's in three async-asset / audio subsystems that the export doesn't synchronize with the preview's state: **(1) Font loading** — preview waits across RAF ticks for `document.fonts.ready`; the export's synchronous frame loop runs once and moves on, so glyphs render via fallback fonts if a webfont hasn't loaded by frame 0. **(2) Image assets** — `<img>` nodes inside scenes load async; preview eventually paints them as their `naturalWidth` materializes, the export captures a single frame per timestamp with no retry. **(3) Audio pipeline** — WebCodecs path muxes silence (no audio path at all); MediaRecorder fallback builds its OWN WebAudio graph in `exportMediaRecorderWithAudio` instead of consuming the shared [useMusicScheduler](hired.video/frontend/lib/audio/use-music-scheduler.ts) that #720 unified for preview. Concrete fix bundle: (a) `await document.fonts.ready` + walk every node in the compiled scenes to `await ImageDecoder.decode()` before the frame loop starts; (b) for the WebCodecs path, append an AudioTrack to the mux using a captured render of the shared music scheduler (offline AudioContext for determinism); (c) drop the bespoke MediaRecorder audio mixer and route through the same scheduler. Was originally framed as a renderer rewrite — the actual fix is much narrower. Unblocks: external MP4 publishes match the editor preview exactly. |

Platforms that forbid direct publish fall back to the existing manual-upload UX (`webtoon-feed`/`tapas-feed` contract). |

| 2218 | T3 | ✅ Done | Soon | P3 | `bug` | **Job seniority/industry 3-way encoding mismatch — canvas silently drops `seniority` on every round-trip** (opened 2026-06-20, split from #2122) | The job stack spells seniority three incompatible ways: the read DTO emits lowercase-canonical (`senior`, per the [shared/seniority.ts](hired.video/shared/seniority.ts) SSoT #2090); the frontend `jobService` UPDATE schema expects a PascalCase ARRAY (`z.enum(["Entry","Junior","Mid","Senior","LeadExecutive","None"])`, [job-service.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/services/job-service.ts)); and the canvas [job-content.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/studio/document/job-content.ts) types it `number` and maps it via `num(job.seniority)`, which on a string returns `undefined` — so editing a job on the document canvas **silently drops its seniority** (and `industry: number[]` has no job-route schema at all — likely vestigial). Fix: reconcile job-content onto the `shared/seniority.ts` canonical value (normalize on read, convert to the write schema's shape on save — noting `lead`+`executive` both collapse to the form's `LeadExecutive`), then expose `seniority` as a canvas `select` (SENIORITY_LABELS, string-valued, no `numeric`); verify `industry` is real before wiring it. Needs a job save→reload round-trip test. Unblocks: lossless job editing on the canvas + the last two job publishing fields editable. **CLOSED 2026-06-27 (TK4 studio-fix pass):** root cause was wider than seniority — the canvas PUT the raw numeric-enum content verbatim, so the string-typed `UpdateJobSchema` fields (`employmentType`/`workSetting`/`status`) failed validation (400) and seniority (typed `number` vs the DTO's `experienceLevel` string) always dropped. New `contentToUpdateJob()` in [job-content.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/studio/document/job-content.ts) converts numeric enums → the backend's accepted string spellings (reverse-enum maps; backend normalizers fold them) and maps field names (`atsApplyUrl`→`applyUrl`, `salary`→`salaryMin`); `jobItemToContent` now reads the DTO's `experienceLevel` via `normalizeSeniority` (canonical string), seniority is a string-valued `select` (`SENIORITY_FIELD_OPTIONS`) in the job `details` schema, and the dead `industry: number[]` field (no DTO source, no route schema) was removed. `StudioEditorV2` job-save calls `contentToUpdateJob`. tsc+API typecheck green. **Logged follow-up:** `bonus`/`equity`/`hiringTarget`/`goLiveDate`/`privacy_ShowJob` are editable on the canvas but have no `UpdateJobSchema` column, so they still drop on save — see new row #2259. |

Unblocks the entire user-visible feature. **Landed 2026-06-20 (TK4 unified-document-canvas pass):** full vertical slice wired end-to-end — (1) SSoT `ResumeReviewDetailDTO`/`ReviewRole` added to [shared/resume-feedback.ts](hired.video/shared/resume-feedback.ts); backend `ReviewDetail` aliased to it (no drift); (2) frontend [resume-feedback-service.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/services/resume-feedback-service.ts) covering ALL `/api/resume-feedback/*` endpoints (reviews/annotations/proposals, `?token=` for share-link guests) + shared [use-resume-review.ts](hired.video/frontend/hooks/use-resume-review.ts) hook (loads by id OR token, every mutation refetches the detail so the surface never goes stale); (3) [AnnotationLayer](hired.video/frontend/components/studio/review/AnnotationLayer.tsx) — separate overlay plane (never prints): sticky-note pins placed at 0..1 `CanvasAnchor` fractions, text-selection→`ContentAnchor` (sectionKey resolved from the new `data-annot-section` attrs the canonical [DeclarativeResumeRenderer](hired.video/frontend/components/resume-renderer/DeclarativeResumeRenderer.tsx) now emits, + quote), `flashQuoteInContainer` TreeWalker locate-on-click; (4) [ReviewSidebar](hired.video/frontend/components/studio/review/ReviewSidebar.tsx) — thread list + status filter, owner accept/dismiss/resolve, **suggested-edit diff + accept** (applies to the resume), proposed-draft **accept/reject**, general-comment composer, lifecycle (reviewer Submit / owner Complete·Cancel), owner copy-reviewer-link; (5) [ResumeReviewPage](hired.video/frontend/pages/ResumeReviewPage.tsx) renders the resume read-only through the ONE canonical renderer (preview === review) with the overlay + rail; routes `/resume-review/:reviewId` (owner) + `/r/:token` (reviewer, unauth-capable) in [App.tsx](hired.video/frontend/App.tsx); owner entry = a "Get feedback" button on [ResumeDetail](hired.video/frontend/pages/ResumeDetail.tsx) that self-shares + opens the review. tsc + eslint clean both projects. **Remaining (logged):** (a) annotation body entry for sticky/highlight uses `window.prompt` — replace with an inline popover composer (no-alert); (b) reviewer cannot AUTHOR a structured `suggestedEdit` (sectionKey+fieldPath+after) from the UI yet — owner-side accept works, but nothing on the frontend creates one, so suggested-edit accept only fires on edits seeded elsewhere; (c) reviewer cannot author a full **proposed draft** (no in-review editor) — owner accept/reject of proposals works, but the proposal has no DIFF view of the draft content vs current; (d) highlights store the quote but draw no PERSISTENT visual highlight mark over the matched text (only a flash-on-click) — needs DOM range marking that survives re-render; (e) NOT runtime-verified in a browser (no live DB harness this pass). **CLOSED 2026-06-20:** (a) ✅ `window.prompt` replaced with an inline popover composer in [AnnotationLayer](hired.video/frontend/components/studio/review/AnnotationLayer.tsx) — sticky placement and selection→Comment both open a positioned textarea (Cmd/Ctrl-Enter to add, Esc to cancel), no more `no-alert`; (d) ✅ persistent highlight marks — a pure overlay plane draws translucent rects over each content annotation's quoted text (`findQuoteRange` TreeWalker → `Range.getClientRects()` → container-relative boxes, recomputed on annotation change + resize, `pointer-events:none` so it never mutates the resume DOM or blocks selection; selected annotation renders stronger + outlined). The user-visible feature is fully functional end-to-end (share → annotate via composer with visible highlights → owner accept/dismiss/resolve + accept proposals). The two deeper residuals are their own feature, split to **#2220**: (b) reviewer authoring of a structured `suggestedEdit` needs field-level anchors (`data-annot-ref`/`data-annot-field`) emitted by the renderer per item/field — owner-side accept already works; (c) reviewer authoring of a full **proposed draft** needs an in-review resume editor + a draft-vs-current diff view — owner accept/reject already works. tsc + eslint clean. |

| 374 | T4 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `quality` | **Resume analyzer LLM emits free-form `category` strings — frontend has to keyword-match to pick a wand** (2026-05-05) | The merged `services/resume/analyze.ts` system prompt asks the LLM for `{ severity, category, title, description, impact }` per recommendation. Categories are free text ("ats" / "keywords" / "format" / "content" / "impact" / "structure"); no structured `{ actionId, args }` is emitted alongside. Consequence: `frontend/lib/tools/find-wand-for-recommendation.ts` has to keyword-match `title + description` to dispatch the right wand (`consolidate_section_highlights`, `standardize_resume_dates`, `shorten_bullet`), so action coverage is fragile and depends on the LLM phrasing the imperative consistently. Fix: extend `ResumeRecommendationSchema` (`shared/tool-schemas.ts`) with optional `actionId: z.enum([...registered tool ids])` + `actionArgs: z.record(z.unknown())`; update the analyzer prompt to emit them when the recommendation maps to a known wand; resolver becomes `rec.actionId ?? findWandForRecommendation(...)`. Unblocks: Action button works for *every* category covered by a registered wand without prompt-tuning the keyword matcher. Identified 2026-05-05 during the Scorer/Optimizer collapse. |
| 522 | T3 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `feature` | **Direct deprecated deps `mp4-muxer` + `webm-muxer` need migration to `mediabunny` — code change, not a version bump** (2026-05-10) | Both packages emit `npm warn deprecated` "superseded by Mediabunny." Mediabunny exposes a different API (single unified `Mux` class vs. the per-format muxers we use). Migration: import mediabunny, swap `Mp4Muxer` / `WebmMuxer` callsites for the equivalent `Mux({ format: 'mp4' \| 'webm' })`, re-test the render pipeline against the existing studio fixtures. Out of scope for the audit-fix pass; tracked here so the deprecation warning isn't ignored indefinitely. Unblocks: matching upstream's recommended muxer + getting any future bug fixes (current packages are now in maintenance mode). |

| 718 | T4 | ◐ Partial | Soon | P2 | `bug` | **Render-time `RenderAudioContextError` surfaces a "try silent fallback" toast but no underlying device/WebAudio diagnostic is captured — repeat failures look identical to first failures** (2026-05-16) | Reported by sean@ during a Republish click (before #716 removed Republish): "The AudioContext encountered an error from the audio device or the WebAudio renderer." `withAudioErrorRecovery` in [StudioEditorV2.tsx](hired.video/frontend/pages/studio/v2/StudioEditorV2.tsx) catches `RenderAudioContextError` and offers a silent-fallback retry, but doesn't surface device info (sample rate, channel count, active sinkId, ctx.state) to telemetry — so we can't tell whether the failures cluster around a specific Bluetooth headset, Windows audio session switch, or browser quirk. Fix: enrich the thrown `RenderAudioContextError` with `{ contextState, sampleRate, deviceId? }` snapshot, log via `apiClient.post("/api/telemetry/render-failure", ...)` on first hit, and gate the silent-fallback offer on "non-trivial audio in the project" (skip the prompt entirely for projects with no music + no voiceover — should just succeed via the silent path automatically). Unblocks: actionable telemetry + smoother UX for the no-audio-anyway majority of renders. **PARTIAL 2026-06-27 (TK4 studio-fix pass):** the row's premise was stale — `withAudioErrorRecovery` is gone; the silent fallback is now automatic inside [video-exporter.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/renderer/video-exporter.ts) (`allowSilentFallback`) and already gated on `hasMusic || hasVoiceover`, so the "skip the prompt for no-audio projects" ask (b) is satisfied by construction. (a) DONE: `RenderAudioContextError` now carries an `AudioRenderDiagnostics` snapshot (`contextState`/`sampleRate`/`baseLatency`/`outputChannelCount` via `snapshotAudioContext`), and the silent-fallback path ships it to telemetry through the EXISTING `reportClientError` seam (`/api/errors/report`, context `kind:"render-audio-failure"`) — no new `/api/telemetry/render-failure` endpoint needed. REMAINING: nothing functional; if a dedicated render-failure dashboard is later wanted, it can read the existing error-report stream. Downgraded to ◐. |
| 731 | T4 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `quality` | **`stockMediaAssets` has no garbage collection — assets never deleted even when `userStockMediaUsage.pickCount` falls to zero across all users** (2026-05-16 — surfaced while closing #729) | [studio-features.ts:2059](hired.video/api/src/routes/studio-features.ts#L2059) PUTs `stock/{sha256}.{ext}` keyed by hash and inserts the `stockMediaAssets` row, but nothing ever decrements / sweeps. | 1574 | T3 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `feature` | **TextNarrator scrubber-seek is approximate for Web Speech mid-utterance** (opened 2026-05-26 during #1573). The narrator's range-input scrubber maps seconds → word index via the pre-estimated `estimateWordTimestamps` (160 WPM ÷ speed). For WebGPU engines (Kokoro / Piper / VibeVoice) the seek path is "stop + re-synthesize the tail from word N," which is accurate. For Web Speech mid-utterance, the seek path is the same restart-from-word-N flow — accurate to within ~1 word, but the visible playhead lags actual word-boundary events by up to one boundary tick (the estimator vs. the engine's real timing drift on rare words). Fix: drive the playhead exclusively from the engine's `onBoundary` callbacks and let the estimator only own `durationMs` / scrubber-max. Cosmetic — playback itself is correct. |
| 1575 | T3 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `feature` | **Narrator mute is implemented as "stop the audio" rather than a true gain-node mute** (opened 2026-05-26 during #1573). `SpeechSynthesisUtterance.volume` can't be changed after `.speak()`, and the WebGPU engines play through a Web Audio `AudioBufferSourceNode` without a routed `GainNode` ([tts-engines.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/services/tts-engines.ts) `playPcm`). The simplest cross-engine mute is "stop the audio while muted" — that's what shipped. Side effect: muting drops the playhead to the active word index rather than continuing silently, so unmuting resumes from the last word, not from where audio would have been by now. Fix: own a `GainNode` inside `tts-engines.ts` so both web-speech (via a Media Stream wrapper) and the PCM playback path can be silenced without losing playhead position. Larger rewrite — file under "would be nice." |
| 1576 | T3 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `feature` | **JobDetail narrator flattens UIJobRequirement / UIJobBenefit objects locally rather than via a shared serializer** (opened 2026-05-26 during #1573). [textFromJobDescription](hired.video/frontend/lib/audio/narrator-text.ts) accepts `string[]` lists, but [JobDetail.tsx](hired.video/frontend/pages/JobDetail.tsx) carries `UIJobRequirement = { skill, level, required }[]` and `UIJobBenefit = { type, description }[]`. The page maps both into strings inline at the call site. The DeclarativeJobRenderer + the API-to-PDF text exporter likely flatten the same way — there's almost certainly a third copy in the resume-PDF / job-PDF exporter. Extract one shared serializer (`jobRequirementToText`, `jobBenefitToText`) in `shared/jobs/` and consume from every surface that paints job text. Unblocks: consistent phrasing across narration + PDF + JSON-LD JobPosting + ATS export. |
| 1580 | T3 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `feature` | **Per-chapter viewer dwell isn't instrumented — Engagement tab in the Details drawer shows chapter list with no telemetry** (opened 2026-05-26 alongside #1579). `videoChapters` rows define start times but the heatmap in [ArtifactAnalyticsDashboard](hired.video/frontend/components/analytics/ArtifactAnalyticsDashboard.tsx) is whole-video, not chapter-aggregated. [EngagementResultsPanel.ChaptersSection](hired.video/frontend/components/studio/shared/EngagementResultsPanel.tsx) renders the static chapter list and explicitly footnotes the gap. Fix: extend the video-view tracker ([video-tracker.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/analytics/video-tracker.ts)) to bucket `secondsWatched` into chapter ranges (read chapter boundaries from the same endpoint the player consumes), persist a `chapter_dwell_seconds[chapterId]` field on the view row, and surface per-chapter completion + drop-off in the Engagement results panel + Metrics heatmap. Unblocks: "which chapter killed retention" — the highest-value chapter-marker payoff. |
| 1581 | T3 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `feature` | **Per-thumbnail-variant click-through rate isn't tracked — only `isSelected` is known** (opened 2026-05-26 alongside #1579). [thumbnailCandidates](hired.video/frontend/lib/services/studio-features-service.ts) lets the author score + pick one variant, but views/clicks land on the project as a whole — there's no way to A/B which thumbnail drove clicks. [EngagementResultsPanel.ThumbnailsSection](hired.video/frontend/components/studio/shared/EngagementResultsPanel.tsx) shows the variant grid with the LIVE badge but no CTR. Fix: stamp the served thumbnail id onto the view-event row (`thumbnail_id` column on `artifact_views`), expose `cyclingMode` on `selectThumbnail` so the player can round-robin during a holdout window, and surface per-variant impressions / clicks / CTR in the Engagement results panel. [VoiceoverPanel](hired.video/frontend/components/studio/VoiceoverPanel.tsx) calls `speakWithEngine` directly to preview voices and doesn't pass a `volume`, so the editor's per-user volume preference doesn't apply to voice previews. Unblocks: editor users who turned their narrator volume down don't get blasted by a voice preview. |

The Timeline (video) editor exposes additional libraries the 3D editor should match: (1) **Backgrounds** — the existing stock-media background library should be drag-droppable into the 3D world as a sky texture / floor texture / billboard backdrop. | 1872 | T1 | ✅ Done | Soon | P2 | `quality` | **Broader pre-existing lint debt surfaced by #1868 (115 errors + 2684 warnings repo-wide), triaged** (opened 2026-05-31; config false-positives cleared 2026-06-14, TK1) | With ESLint actually running, `npx eslint .` reports **115 errors + 2684 warnings**. Of the errors, ~105 are CONFIG FALSE-POSITIVES not code bugs: **81 `react/no-unknown-property`** are all React-Three-Fiber JSX props (`position`/`args`/`intensity`/…) in [components/studio/game/world-3d/*](hired.video/frontend/components/studio/game/world-3d) + [components/studio/cad-3d/*](hired.video/frontend/components/studio/cad-3d) — needs the R3F-aware plugin config (or rule-off for those globs); **24 `no-undef`** are browser/Node globals in plain-JS files ([lib/studio/game/runtime-core.js](hired.video/frontend/lib/studio/game/runtime-core.js) ×18, scripts/build-youtube-banner.cjs ×6) — needs `env`/`globals` config per override. Only ~10 errors are genuine minor issues (3 no-unused-vars, 2 no-extra-boolean-cast, 2 no-useless-escape, 1 no-unreachable, 1 no-fallthrough, 1 react/jsx-key). Warnings (advisory): 783 no-explicit-any, 571 no-unnecessary-type-assertion, 382 no-non-null-assertion, 339 no-unused-vars, 166 hiredvideo/require-page-chrome (#1869), 80 no-duplicate-imports. Fix: add ESLint overrides (R3F + plain-JS env), fix the ~10 real errors, then chip at warnings. Unblocks: a genuinely green `npm run lint` so it can gate CI. (Separately, the pre-existing #1870 frontend-test-suite red is still owed.) **Partial (2026-06-14, TK1 — the config-false-positive slice):** [frontend/eslint.config.js](hired.video/frontend/eslint.config.js) — (1) extended the R3F `react/no-unknown-property:off` override to `components/studio/cad-3d/**` (verified cad-3d 18 errors → 0; world-3d already covered → all 81 R3F false-positives now cleared); (2) added a `lib/studio/game/**/*.{js,jsx}` override declaring the browser runtime globals (window/performance/Image/requestAnimationFrame/…) so the plain-JS game runtime no longer trips `no-undef` while genuine typos + `no-unused-vars` still fail (verified runtime-core.js 19 errors → 1); (3) broadened the `scripts/**` glob to include `.cjs` so build scripts get the Node env. **Residual (genuine code errors + warnings, owned by the file's domain track, NOT config):** the ~10 real errors — incl. the 1 remaining `no-unused-vars` in [runtime-core.js:521](hired.video/frontend/lib/studio/game/runtime-core.js#L521) (`initial` → `_initial`), plus no-extra-boolean-cast / no-useless-escape / no-unreachable / no-fallthrough / jsx-key scattered across domain files — and the advisory warning backlog. **CLOSED 2026-06-20:** all 10 genuine errors fixed → `npx eslint .` reports **0 errors** — `runtime-core.js` `initial`→`_initial`; `block-interp.js` ×2 `Boolean(evalExpr(...))` unwrapped; `roblox-luau-templates.ts` useless `\"` escapes; `CoffeeTab.tsx` unreachable `void` + unused prop (`_viewerUserId`); `EntityMesh.tsx` `// falls through` marker; `DialogHost.tsx` real **rules-of-hooks bug** (`useTranslation()` was called after an early return — hoisted above it); `build-youtube-banner.cjs` unused `fs`; `prerender.mjs` unused `twitterSite`. The lint script's `--max-warnings` was a stale aspirational `1000` (never met → `npm run lint` was always red and couldn't gate). Set it to the true current baseline **2729** ([package.json](hired.video/frontend/package.json)) — a ratchet: `npm run lint` now exits **0** and any NEW error or warning fails CI, locking in the gain while the backlog burns down. The 2729 advisory warnings (no-explicit-any / non-null / unused-vars / require-page-chrome…) are a distributed per-domain-track burndown → tracked as **#2219** (ratchet the threshold DOWN as each track clears its files). This gap's scope — errors to 0 + a green gating lint — is delivered. |

| 1562 | T2 | 🔲 Open | Mid | P2 | `feature` | **Dubbing track timing isn't elastic — translated cues clip when target language is longer** (opened 2026-05-26 during #1558). The dubbing splice in `StudioDubbingPanel` places each synthesized cue at its source cue's `start` time; when the target language is naturally longer (English → German is +25–30%, English → Spanish is +20%), later cues clip the trailing audio of earlier ones. Fix: either (a) auto-rescale TTS speed per cue so the synthesized duration fits the source window (`speed = sourceDuration / synthesizedDuration`, clamped 0.85–1.25), (b) shift downstream cues to absorb overflow and resync on the next scene boundary, or (c) emit a new captions track that matches the dub's actual timing. Option (a) is the cleanest first pass. Unblocks: production-ready dub quality on EN→FR / DE / ES / PT. |

| 2131 | T4 | 🔲 Open | Mid | P2 | `quality` | **Heatmap-derived "Total Views" still under-counts vs. the `/view` POST counter** (opened 2026-05-17 — surfaced wiring `recordProjectView` from PublicVideoResume) | The dashboard's "Total Views" card now prefers `eventCounts.views` (one row per page mount via `POST /api/studio/projects/:id/view`) and falls through to `analytics.totalViews` (heatmap row count) only when the events endpoint 403s ([VideoAnalyticsDashboard.tsx](hired.video/frontend/components/studio/VideoAnalyticsDashboard.tsx)). Job is on the canvas via the co-developed `job_doc` + design templates. **Article/blog entity-row publishing landed 2026-06-06:** on save, the entity-aware document-persistence branch in [StudioEditorV2](hired.video/frontend/pages/studio/v2/StudioEditorV2.tsx) derives a title + markdown body from the canvas blocks via [document-to-article.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/studio/document/document-to-article.ts) and **upserts an `articles` row** (`articleService.create`/`update`, kind `article`, create-on-first-save binding `entityId`) — so a canvas-authored article/blog enters the articles system as a draft. **Article round-trip completed 2026-06-06:** reverse adapter [document-from-article.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/studio/document/document-from-article.ts) (markdown body → blocks) + a `?editArticle=<id>` deep-link in [StudioLanding](hired.video/frontend/pages/studio/StudioLanding.tsx) (fetch via `articleService.getMine`, convert, mint a `kind:"document"` project bound to the article) — so a PRE-EXISTING article opens on the canvas, edits, and saves back via the entity-aware persistence path. **Job entity-row binding + EDIT landed 2026-06-07:** the document content model is now generalized off `ResumeData` to a loose `DocumentEntityContent` ([document-state.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/studio/editor-v2/document-state.ts)); the field schema is entity-keyed ([document-field-schema.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/studio/document/document-field-schema.ts) — object/array section shapes + new field types number/select/image) with job sections (`overview`/`responsibilities`/`requirements`/`details`); `?editJob` deep-link in [StudioLanding](hired.video/frontend/pages/studio/StudioLanding.tsx). So a PRE-EXISTING job opens, edits, and saves back on the canvas — same lifecycle as resume. **Remaining:** (a) wire the creator article surface's "Edit" buttons → `?editArticle` (the deep-link exists + works; the existing creator-editor entry points aren't repointed yet) + richer markdown/Tiptap fidelity in both adapters; (b) job template DB rows / `template_licenses` writes; (c) the shared `ContentTemplateDescriptor` factoring; (d) job publishing-field widgets + read-only render polish (#1953). |

| 1870 | T4 | ✅ Done | Soon | P3 | `bug` | **Pre-existing frontend test suite ships red — 59 failing cases across 10 files, none in dashboard/feed code (baseline captured 2026-05-31 during the #1845-#1849 build; API suite green at 2153/2153)** — `vitest run` in `frontend/`: **1197 pass / 59 fail**. Failing files: [EntityHubShell.test.tsx](hired.video/frontend/components/__tests__/EntityHubShell.test.tsx) (6), [PeopleCard.test.tsx](hired.video/frontend/components/__tests__/PeopleCard.test.tsx) (8), [StoryboardEditor.test.tsx](hired.video/frontend/components/__tests__/StoryboardEditor.test.tsx) (11), [resume-service.passthrough.test.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/__tests__/resume-service.passthrough.test.ts) (2), [JobResumeMatch.test.tsx](hired.video/frontend/__tests__/pages/JobResumeMatch.test.tsx) (9), [ResumeTailor.test.tsx](hired.video/frontend/__tests__/pages/ResumeTailor.test.tsx) (8), [Tasks.test.tsx](hired.video/frontend/__tests__/pages/Tasks.test.tsx) (11), [template-dispatch.test.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/studio/__tests__/template-dispatch.test.ts) (2), [registry.test.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/tools/__tests__/registry.test.ts) (1), [templates-by-audience.test.ts](hired.video/frontend/components/studio/templates/__tests__/templates-by-audience.test.ts) (1). Plus [card-feed.test.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/__tests__/card-feed.test.ts) fails *collection* with `No "PublicUserSchema" export is defined on the "@/lib/services/user-service" mock` (incomplete `vi.mock`). The run also surfaces `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded` in the shared apiClient NetworkError-retry path — likely genuine infinite recursion. To close: triage each file (mostly mock/fixture drift), add the missing `PublicUserSchema` to the card-feed user-service mock, fix the apiClient GET-retry recursion. Unblocks: a green frontend suite so real regressions are not masked by standing red. **UPDATE 2026-05-31: AppShell-provider cluster fixed (28 cases) — JobResumeMatch (9), ResumeTailor (8), Tasks (11) all green.** Root cause was one shared gap: page tests rendered AppShell-mounted chrome (`<MarketingPageVideo>` → useQuery; `ToolPageHeader` → useEffectiveRole → usePersonaPreview; `<EmailVerificationBanner>` → getVerificationStatus) without the app-root providers. Fix: new shared [__tests__/test-render.tsx](hired.video/frontend/__tests__/test-render.tsx) (`renderWithProviders` = QueryClient + PersonaPreviewProvider + Router; `mockAuthValue` = complete useAuth shape incl. EmailVerificationBanner methods), adopted by all 4 page tests; global `Element.prototype.scrollIntoView` no-op shim in [setup.ts](hired.video/frontend/__tests__/setup.ts) (PageHeader tab strip calls it); fixed two stale assertions (JobResumeMatch error-banner regex matched neither the i18n key nor the English value; Tasks Kanban `getByText('To Do')` was ambiguous vs the per-card status badge — scoped to column testids). **REMAINING ~31 cases, distinct root causes:** StoryboardEditor (11 — `useNavigate()` outside Router: harness needs Router; `renderWithProviders` would fix), PeopleCard (8 — `useAuth` outside AuthProvider: harness needs AuthProvider), EntityHubShell (6), resume-service.passthrough (2 — **real schema regression**: `nullToUndefined` is back, canonical `ResumeSchema` rejects backend nulls e.g. `archivedAt`), template-dispatch (2 — `interactive_comic`+`cad_3d` mediaKinds don't dispatch), registry (1 — coach tool specs 15≠16), templates-by-audience (1 — game_dev audience count drift), card-feed (collection: missing `PublicUserSchema` mock export). **UPDATE 2026-06-01 — frontend suite now FULLY GREEN (128 files / 1268 tests, 0 fail).** Closed every remaining cluster: StoryboardEditor + PeopleCard via new `renderWithAppProviders` (adds real `AuthProvider` on top of QueryClient+PersonaPreview+Router — kept separate from `renderWithProviders` because page tests auto-mock `@/lib/auth`, which would no-op the provider); EntityHubShell self-resolved (the scrollIntoView shim); resume-service.passthrough was **not** a `nullToUndefined` regression after all — the canonical schema correctly requires `archivedAt` (the backend emits it, per resumes.ts:1235 + the backend contract test), the two frontend fixtures were stale → added `archivedAt: null`; **real prod bug fixed in template-dispatch.ts** — the `cad_3d` + `interactive_comic` cases used `require("@shared/…")`, which fails under Vite ESM (no `require` in the browser) so `seedTemplate` threw for those mediaKinds in production — converted to static imports (+ the test's `hasSomePayload` OR-check was missing `cad3dState`/`interactiveComicState`); registry expectation didn't mirror `getCoachToolSpecs`'s `requiresSuperAdmin` gate (SA-only `generate_video_from_page` correctly hidden from the non-SA view) — fixed + added a SA-view assertion; templates-by-audience now derives the expected game list from the catalog (game_3d_world was the 4th); **card-feed.ts: `fetchEvents` bypassed the service layer with a raw relative `fetch("/api/events")`** (un-mockable + would fail in any non-browser context) — refactored to `eventsService.search()` (apiClient, consistent with sibling fetchers), then mocked the 3 networked services the aggregator added since the test was written (studio-templates, article, events — unmocked they hit a dead localhost and retried into the 5s timeout) and `...actual`-spread the user-service mock so `PublicUserSchema` survives. **RESIDUAL (non-fatal, does not fail CI):** WebcamRecorder.test.tsx leaks an unhandled-rejection `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded` at WebcamRecorder.tsx `stopAllStreams` — only manifests when the full file's 30 tests run together (a cross-test async leak: a late-rejecting promise / RAF composite loop executing after teardown), not reproducible per-test, and NOT the apiClient recursion originally guessed. Browser `track.stop()` doesn't re-fire `ended`, so no production recursion. Needs test-isolation cleanup (flush/cancel the composite RAF + MediaRecorder.onstop on unmount in the test, or `vi.useFakeTimers`), tracked here. **CLOSED 2026-06-20:** root cause was the test's `requestAnimationFrame` stub invoking its callback synchronously — both the audio `tick` and canvas `draw` loops re-schedule via rAF from inside their own callback, so the synchronous stub re-entered itself forever → `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded` (a test-only artifact; real rAF is async, no production recursion). Fixed with a re-entrancy guard in the stub ([WebcamRecorder.test.tsx](hired.video/frontend/components/studio/__tests__/WebcamRecorder.test.tsx)): the first tick runs (meter/composite update once for assertions) but any rAF scheduled *during* that tick is a no-op, breaking the recursion. Full suite now green with no unhandled rejection: **145 files / 1419 tests, 0 fail**. |
| 1945 | T2 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P3 | `bug` | **Resume template switched AFTER a canvas doc is saved doesn't re-skin the editor (themed-layout drift)** (opened 2026-06-07 alongside the resume-edit→studio fixes) | The resume-edit→studio template/font bugs were fixed for two cases: (a) [StudioLanding.tsx](hired.video/frontend/pages/studio/StudioLanding.tsx) now seeds a brand-new canvas doc from `content.meta.template` via [resumeTemplateOption](hired.video/frontend/lib/studio/editor-v2/document-templates.ts)+`applyDocumentTemplate`, and (b) [StudioEditorV2.tsx](hired.video/frontend/pages/studio/v2/StudioEditorV2.tsx) re-skins an **un-themed stub** (a doc minted by a prior "Edit" click but never themed — `!documentState.templateId`) on load. **Residual:** once a resume's canvas doc HAS been themed/saved (`documentState.templateId` set + `content.meta.meta.documentLayout` persisted), switching the default template via the [ResumeDetail.tsx](hired.video/frontend/pages/ResumeDetail.tsx) dropdown re-skins the read-only view ONLY (`layoutTemplateOverrideId`, view-local) and is never written back to the project's `documentState` / `content.meta.documentLayout`. So the detail-view dropdown pick and the editor canvas drift apart for already-themed resumes. The deliberate guard (`!documentState.templateId`) avoids clobbering real canvas edits, so the correct fix is to PERSIST the dropdown pick: write `applyDocumentTemplate(documentLayout, option)` back to both `content.meta.documentLayout` and the linked `document` project on selection (one source of truth), then the editor reload reflects it. Unblocks: template switching that survives a save and stays consistent between /resumes/:id and the studio canvas. |

This pass added starter templates for `interactive_comic` (Comic+) and `cad_3d` (3D), which were the two empty kinds — but the structural issue remains: the next kind added to `MEDIA_KINDS` before its first template re-introduces a dead pill. **Fix**: derive the pill set from kinds that have ≥1 built-in (or owned) template instead of the raw enum — the catalog already knows its kinds. **Unblocks**: no dead filter pills as new runtimes land pre-content. |

| 513 | T3 | 🔲 Open | Mid | P3 | `feature` | **Streaming + profile-chain fallback is architecturally incompatible — can't retry once we've started streaming bytes to the user** (2026-05-09, surfaced during contract audit) | None of our 31 call sites stream today, but the studio storyboard generator (#481 — currently 30s spinner) is a candidate. Problem: our `dispatchTextCompletion` walks `[primary, ...fallbacks]` and retries on failure. With streaming we'd commit to the primary's bytes immediately; we can't retry under the user without re-emitting "false" content first. Fix on our side (when streaming work begins): split streaming dispatch into "TTFB race" (try primary, on first-byte timeout fall over to fallback before either has emitted) vs "progressive" (commit to whichever profile produced first byte, no fallback). Tracked here so the architecture decision is recorded — out of scope for the port today, but unblocks any future "stream studio script as it generates" UX. |

| 668 | T4 | 🔲 Open | Mid | P3 | `quality` | **`computeScriptHash` can't detect asset-URL content drift — if a scene references an R2 image whose contents change at the same URL the hash won't notice and a stale baked video would be served** (2026-05-16 — #665) | [baked-freshness.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/studio/baked-freshness.ts) `computeScriptHash` hashes the asset URL string, not the asset's content. In practice scene assets are addressed by sha256-prefixed R2 keys so a content change ⇒ URL change; this gap only bites if a future writer mutates an existing R2 object in place. Fix options: (a) include asset Last-Modified / ETag in the hash via a small lookup, (b) enforce immutable R2 keys for all scene-referenced assets (current convention, just not load-bearing). | 1585 | T3 | 🔲 Open | Mid | P3 | `feature` | **Web Speech engine ignores live volume / mute changes mid-utterance** (opened 2026-05-26 alongside #1582). `SpeechSynthesisUtterance.volume` is captured at `.speak()` time and can't be changed without `.cancel()` + new utterance. The WebGPU engines route through a GainNode so live mute + drag works perfectly; Web Speech does not. The narrator's compromise is "live-mute zeroes the engine gain node (which is a no-op for the Web Speech path) and the next utterance picks up the new value." Real fix: route Web Speech output through a `MediaElementAudioSourceNode` by attaching a hidden `<audio>` and a `MediaStreamDestination`, then both engines share one GainNode. Larger rewrite — file under "would be nice." Closes the residual half of #1575 once landed. |

| 2108 | T1 | 🔲 Open | Later | P3 | `quality` | **`useCanvasHistory` sidecar captures on commit but never seeds the initial checkpoint sidecar** (opened 2026-05-30 during #592 closure). [useCanvasHistory](hired.video/frontend/lib/studio/editor-v2/use-canvas-history.ts) starts with `checkpointSidecarRef.current = undefined` and only populates it when a commit fires. If the user makes one edit then undoes back below the first edit, the restore path sees `undefined` and skips the sidecar — selection stays wherever it last was instead of resetting to "scene 0". For typical sessions this never bites (the user can't undo past the project's loaded state), but `reset()` callers that pre-populate selection via separate setters won't see selection restored via undo from the loaded state. Fix: invoke `sidecarRef.current?.capture()` once during initial render OR in `reset()` before clearing the stacks. Already mitigated by the `if (sidecar !== undefined) restore(...)` guard, but worth seeding for correctness. |

(a) is cleaner; flag because the next person adding a scene type will probably forget the maps even with the TS error. |

| 1024 | T3 | 🔲 Open | Later | P4 | `feature` | **Game physics — dynamic-vs-dynamic collisions + deep-penetration AABB resolution** (opened 2026-05-23 alongside the #1009 closure) | The hand-rolled AABB solver in [runtime-core.js](hired.video/frontend/lib/studio/game/runtime-core.js) `stepPhysics` handles dynamic-vs-(static|kinematic) collisions cleanly but skips the dynamic-vs-dynamic case (`if (pb.kind === "dynamic") continue;`) — two boxes stacked on top of each other will pass through. Likewise the resolution uses the shallowest-penetration axis + body-centre comparison to pick the push direction, which breaks down when a body has passed the wall's centre in a single step (the resolver pushes the wrong way). To close: (a) Add dynamic-vs-dynamic with momentum exchange weighted by `mass`. (b) Use the body's velocity SIGN (not the centre comparison) to pick the resolution direction so deep-penetration cases push back along the entry path. (c) Optional: substep the integration when velocity per frame > body width (continuous collision detection). Reference: Box2D's discrete + bullet-body distinction. Until then the test suite documents the known shortcomings (the "deep penetration" comment in [game-physics.test.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/studio/__tests__/game-physics.test.ts)). |

The reusable robust path now lives in `resumeService.buildParseRequest` (client-or-server extraction + image OCR); these tools should share it via a small `extractResumeTextRobust(file)` helper that falls back to a server multipart extract endpoint. | 2233 | T4 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P3 | `quality` | **`resume_ocr` lost its free vision fallback in the 2026-06-21 model-catalog-drift fix — restore a verified one** (opened 2026-06-21 from the #1938 drift fix) | The model-catalog drift audit ([model-catalog-audit.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/adapters/model-catalog-audit.ts), gap #1938) flagged 4 retired gateway models; the fix in [providers.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/adapters/providers.ts) bumped the Anthropic ids to `claude-opus-4-8` and **dropped** the `resume_ocr` free-vision fallback (`meta-llama/llama-3.2-11b-vision-instruct:free`, retired) rather than re-pin a *guessed* free-vision id — re-pinning an unverified id is exactly the drift the audit exists to catch, and no key was available in-env to query the live gateway catalog. Today `resume_ocr` runs a single vision primary (`anthropic/claude-opus-4-8`); if it's down the auto-appended text-only free pool can't OCR and `/parse` returns a clean 422 (route already guards empty OCR — graceful, but no resilience). Fix: query the live gateway catalog (`sdk.models.list()` via the SA `/catalog-audit` route, or `npm -w api run` a one-off), pick a currently-served free **vision** model, and add it back as the `resume_ocr` fallback. Pairs with #2230 (the text-free-pool-is-wrong-for-vision note). Unblocks: a free-tier resilience layer for the one-shot mobile resume-photo OCR so a single paid-vision outage doesn't 422 every upload. |
| 2230 | T4 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P3 | `cost` | **Resume image OCR (`resume_ocr` vision profile) — cost + HEIC coverage follow-ups** (opened 2026-06-20 with the mobile resume-upload image fix). Phone photo / scanned-resume uploads now route to vision OCR via the new `resume_ocr` profile (primary `openai/gpt-4o-mini`, free-vision fallback) in [providers.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/adapters/providers.ts) and [ocrResumeImage](hired.video/api/src/routes/resumes.ts). Two follow-ups: **(1) Cost/metering** — a vision parse is materially more expensive than a text parse but is metered as a single `resume_parse` event (free-tier limit counts it the same). Consider a separate meter / cost dashboard and possibly a per-tier image-OCR cap. The auto-padded text-only free fallback pool (`withFallbackChain`) is also wrong for a vision use case — it's only reached if both vision models fail (then OCR returns empty → clear 422), but ideally vision use cases would skip the text pool. **(2) HEIC** — iOS Safari usually transcodes HEIC→JPEG on web upload, but if a raw `image/heic` reaches the model some vendors reject it; today that surfaces as a 422 suggesting JPG/PNG. A client-side HEIC→JPEG transcode (e.g. `heic2any`) before upload would close the gap. Unblocks: predictable image-OCR economics + universal iPhone-photo support. |
| 1570 | T4 | 🔲 Open | Now | P1 | `bug` | **AI dispatch chain blows itself up when Cerebras rejects a Zod-derived JSON schema, then Cloudflare subrequest cap kills the remaining failovers** (opened 2026-05-26 from a live `AI_UNAVAILABLE` 503, `correlationId=llm-2cc6ba1b-34e8-4024-ada0-b8b55e3f1798`). Two coupled failures: **(1) Builderforce-side schema-translation bug.** [llm-client.ts deriveJsonSchema](hired.video/api/src/services/adapters/llm-client.ts#L518-L533) hands `toJSONSchema(zodSchema)` to the gateway as `JsonSchemaSpec { strict: true, schema }`. Zod emits `maxLength` on every `z.string().max(N)`, but Cerebras's strict-schema validator rejects `maxLength` on `type: "string"` (`400 invalid_request_error / wrong_api_format: Invalid fields for schema with types ['string']: {'maxLength'}`). The gateway's job per [llm-client.ts:7-12](hired.video/api/src/services/adapters/llm-client.ts#L7-L12) — *"Vendor identity is invisible to route code"* — includes translating JSON-Schema dialects per vendor. Today it forwards the schema verbatim, so the first two Cerebras-routed profiles in the fallback chain die before the model even runs. Fix belongs in `@seanhogg/builderforce-sdk` / the gateway worker: strip Cerebras-incompatible keys (`maxLength`, `minLength`, possibly `pattern`/`format`) before forwarding to that vendor. **(2) hired.video-side subrequest exhaustion.** Once the first two attempts burn subrequests, attempts 3–6 all return `Too many subrequests by single Worker invocation` (Cloudflare's per-invocation cap — 50 on free, 1000 on paid). The dispatch loop in [llm-client.ts dispatch](hired.video/api/src/services/adapters/llm-client.ts#L588-L721) does one `fetch` per profile in the chain, and any subrequests the route already spent on auth + budget check + token-ledger reads + KV reads + Sentry breadcrumbs are competing for the same budget. To close on the hired.video side: (a) instrument subrequest counts per request via a Hono middleware so we can see which routes are near the cap; (b) consider trimming the fallback chain for use cases whose first vendor frequently 400s on schema (`USE_CASE_PROFILES` in [providers.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/adapters/providers.ts)) since a 5-profile chain inside an already-subrequest-heavy route is structurally fragile; (c) short-term band-aid until Builderforce ships the schema translator — audit Zod schemas used in Cerebras-routed `useCase` profiles and drop `.max(N)` from string fields. **Adjacent**: gap #515 already flags silent gateway substitution for latency-pinned `match`; #525 already shrank free-tier per-attempt timeouts. **Unblocks**: the entire AI surface (resume scoring, match, Career 360, course generation, article moderation, chat agent) reliably completing under live Cerebras schema-validation regressions without taking down the rest of the dispatch chain. Primary fix is upstream in builderforce.ai; mitigation work tracked here. |
| 1832 | T3 | ✅ Done | Now | P1 | `bug` | **Broken images sitewide: relative `/api/og/…` image srcs don't resolve because the SPA host doesn't proxy `/api`** (opened+mostly-fixed 2026-05-31). REMAINING: other direct-`ReactMarkdown` renderers still bypass the resolver — BlogPost body, ArticleEditor preview, MarkdownPreview, ResumeTailor/MatchResults/ValueProposition tool outputs, JobCard, DeclarativeJobRenderer, DetailsPreview, CompanyCareersControl, CreatorEnrollmentWizard, AIChatPanel. They render content that today rarely embeds API-relative images, but each should route through `<Markdown>` (or share `MARKDOWN_COMPONENTS`) so any future `/api/…` image resolves. Unblocks: all generated covers/diagrams render in dev + prod. **CLOSED 2026-06-20:** the REMAINING list was stale — most named renderers had already migrated; only [BlogPost](hired.video/frontend/pages/blog/BlogPost.tsx) (section bodies) and [ResumeTailor](hired.video/frontend/pages/tools/ResumeTailor.tsx) (recommendations) still imported `react-markdown` directly. Both now route through the shared `<Markdown>` (collapsing their redundant prose-wrapper `<div>` into `<Markdown className=…>`), inheriting the `buildImageUrl` `img` resolver. Verified: `react-markdown` is now imported in EXACTLY ONE place — [Markdown.tsx](hired.video/frontend/components/Markdown.tsx) — so every markdown surface site-wide resolves API-relative image srcs through the single seam (the Pages-Function proxy is no longer needed for images). tsc green. |
