| 507 | T3 | 🔲 Open | Now | P2 | `feature` | **builderforce-sdk contract gap: gateway must NOT make model-selection decisions on our behalf — vendor / model / fallback choice is OUR business logic and stays on our side** (2026-05-09) | First framing of this gap had it backwards (asking the gateway to mirror our `USE_CASE_PROFILES` table). Correct framing: the gateway is a transport, not a policy layer. We keep `USE_CASE_PROFILES` + the fallback chain + the cross-vendor routing decisions on our side ([providers.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/adapters/providers.ts) survives the port; only `openai-compat.ts` + `tryProviderChain` go away). For that to work the SDK must accept and pass through verbatim: (a) explicit `model` per call (no "best available" substitution), (b) `temperature` / `max_tokens` / `top_p` (no clamping), (c) `response_format` typed (#500), (d) `tools` / `tool_choice` typed (#499), (e) any vendor-prefixed model id (`openrouter/...`, `cerebras/...`, `ollama/...`) routed to the correct upstream — we drive routing via the model string, not via gateway intelligence, (f) `useCase` accepted as opaque tenant slug for telemetry only (#501), (g) per-call `metadata` (#504) + `idempotencyKey` (#505) round-tripped on the response. Gateway responsibilities: HTTP transport, vendor authentication, per-tenant rate-limit, server-side observability (`_builderforce.resolvedModel` echo confirming our model string was honored without remapping). Unblocks: clean port that preserves our calibrated model choices + cost/latency/quality tradeoffs; gateway gets to focus on transport reliability rather than maintaining a 32-row product-decision table per tenant. |

| 889 | T4 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `quality` | **Paid Cerebras call (`cerebras/llama3.1-8b`) returned 429 in 615ms via the Builderforce gateway — root cause unknown** (opened 2026-05-21 — surfaced building #888) | The 503 in `correlationId=llm-4c95a353` shows attempt 5 (`cerebras/llama3.1-8b`, `vendor: "cerebras"`) failing with `kind: "rate_limit", status: 429` after 615ms. That's much faster than a real Cerebras-side rate-limit response would surface (TTFT alone is usually ~200ms, plus queueing), suggesting the rejection happened gateway-local — either Builderforce's own per-tenant TPM/RPM cap on Cerebras is set lower than our usage requires, or the gateway's Cerebras account/billing tier is provisioned for a smaller burst than the chain assumes. Either is a config issue, not a code one, but it has to be diagnosed before the new paid-pool terminal in #888 can be relied on as a true safety net. Fix path: (a) reproduce by hitting `/api/admin/impersonate/ai/use-cases` + dispatch a Cerebras-only test from SA tooling, capture the full Builderforce response headers (rate-limit + retry-after), (b) if it's a Builderforce tenant cap, file the SDK gap and upgrade the plan or request a TPM bump; if it's a Cerebras billing tier under Builderforce, escalate to vendor onboarding. Cross-references #515 (Cerebras-only latency contract for `match`) — same vendor surface area. Unblocks: Cerebras stops being a flaky paid fallback; #888's pool can drop to Cerebras-only once headroom is confirmed. |

| 1810 | T4 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P3 | `quality` | **Gateway `traceId` is surfaced on the success path but NOT on the failure path — `LLMDispatchError` still reports a locally-minted `correlationId` (`llm-${crypto.randomUUID()}`) instead of the gateway's authoritative trace** (opened 2026-05-30 while wiring `_builderforce.traceId` from Builderforce SDK 0.8.2 into API responses). On success, [llm-client.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/adapters/llm-client.ts) now threads `_builderforce.traceId` through `LLMUsage.traceId` → `toClientUsage` → the chat + course-generate responses. On failure, `dispatch()` throws `LLMDispatchError` carrying a *client-generated* `correlationId` that was sent **to** the gateway as request metadata — which is NOT the same id the gateway records as its trace. Per the SDK docs the gateway's trace on the error path is `BuilderforceApiError.details.correlationId`; we never read it. Result: a user hitting a 503/502 cannot quote an id that Builderforce support can resolve server-side (the success path can). Fix: in `classifyAttemptError`, capture `(err.details as {correlationId?: string})?.correlationId` onto `LLMDispatchAttempt`, and prefer the gateway trace over the local id when building `LLMDispatchError` (keep the local id only as a fallback for pre-dispatch/non-Builderforce throws). Unblocks: support can trace failed AI calls, not just successful ones. |

| 1649 | T4 | 🔲 Open | Later | P2 | `perf` | **Time-series partitioning for audit-required append-only tables** (opened 2026-05-26 from pass-9 scale audit). `points_ledger`, `point_redemptions`, `usage_ledger`, `xapi_statements`, `admin_audit_log` are append-only with no retention path (financial / compliance audit). At 1M users they'll cross 100M rows within 18 months. Conversion plan per table: (1) CREATE TABLE foo_new (...) PARTITION BY RANGE (created_at); (2) CREATE TABLE foo_yYYYY_mMM PARTITION OF foo_new FOR VALUES FROM ... TO ...; (3) INSERT INTO foo_new SELECT * FROM foo; (4) RENAME; (5) recreate FKs from dependent tables. Risky multi-step migration that requires a maintenance window — deferred until table row counts cross 10M (currently well below). **Unblocks**: parallel scans, efficient retention archive (drop old partition vs. millions of DELETEs), faster query plans at high cardinality. |

| 1631 | T3 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P3 | `feature` | **UnifiedSearch query is exact-substring only — no typo tolerance / stemming / synonyms** (opened 2026-05-26 alongside the static-fetcher push-down fix that made `/?q=research` return the Employer Research tool + the "How to Use the Employer Research Tool" blog post). After this pass, each per-type static fetcher in [card-feed.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/card-feed.ts) filters its in-memory catalogue with a lowercased substring match BEFORE pagination — without this, the recency sort buried relevant articles off page 1 (first 2 articles fetched were always the newest by `publishedAt`, even when none matched the query). `matchesNeedle` is intentionally simple — it doesn't tolerate typos (`reserach` → `research`), doesn't stem (`researching` → `research`), and has no synonym table (`employer` ↔ `company`, `salary` ↔ `compensation` ↔ `pay`, `research` ↔ `audit` ↔ `review`). All three are real user expectations on a search box that says "Search" not "Exact substring search". **Fix path**: (a) cheap win — handcrafted synonym map in `card-feed.ts` for the top ~30 employment-domain pairs, applied on both sides of the match; (b) fuzzy match — wire Fuse.js or a manual Levenshtein-distance-2 check against title/name so single-character typos still hit; (c) stemming — Porter stemmer is ~3kB and tolerates `-ing` / `-ed` / `-s`. None of these are in place today; the current pass deliberately scoped to "fix the literal-match case for the article the user named" and left these as the obvious follow-ups. **Adjacent**: the same fetchers ignore Unicode normalisation, so an accented input or a smart quote doesn't normalise to its ASCII equivalent — surfaces when international content lands on the platform (#1148). |

Plus the compliance + i18n work for the EU market. Doing this BEFORE EU revenue is premature; the schema can land before the infra (no-op while `data_region='us'` is the only value) to avoid a hard cutover. |

An employer who calls `POST /api/account/invite` to bring in recruiters never sees those invitations in Team Members — only the SuperAdmin / legacy `admin` role does. Spec: when the viewer's role is in `canManageUsers` (employer or admin), surface (a) the viewer's own user row, (b) every still-open invitation row whose `invitedBy = viewer.id`, and (c) every user row the viewer invited (resolvable via `invitedBy` once we record it on the accepted user). Today the multi-tenant model is single-account so admin-only is "fine"; once company-scoped membership lands this gap blocks the Team Members surface for employer accounts. [StudioEditorV2.tsx](hired.video/frontend/pages/studio/v2/StudioEditorV2.tsx) was opted out this pass (the file #1860 touched). Unblocks: a green, enforced page-chrome guardrail. **Update 2026-05-31 (KEPT the clean wins, REVERTED the bulk silencing):** kept (1) the composition-aware rule — recognises chrome-provider wrappers (`ToolShell`, `ResumeAnalysisToolPage`, `MarketplaceItemShell`, any `*Layout`) + exempts the admin route-wrapper tree (clears ~46 false positives correctly); (2) the shared `usePageChrome()` + `useDefaultBreadcrumbs()` hooks ([lib/seo.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/seo.ts)) applied to ~20 genuine public pages (real SEO+breadcrumbs). **Reverted** the ~76 bulk `eslint-page-chrome: skip` opt-outs — that was metric-gaming (and the scripted edits broke 4 files' imports, caught by CI). **Set the rule to `warn`** ([eslint.config.js](hired.video/frontend/eslint.config.js)) as the honest interim: the remaining ~99 pages surface as warnings (visible, not blocking) pending a DELIBERATE migration. Recommended end-state: split the rule into "layout chrome everywhere (via composition)" + "SEO/breadcrumbs on public pages only", so authed-app pages need neither an opt-out nor pointless SEO. Remaining work: that split + adding real chrome to the genuine public pages still warning. |

| 2158 | T4 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P3 | `arch` | **`(schema as any).table` casts across route/service files defeat compile-time column-drift detection** (found 2026-06-13 while fixing the `/api/audit/me` 500) | The `/api/audit/me` 500 (`Cannot convert undefined or null to object`) was schema drift: [userAuditLog.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/audit/userAuditLog.ts) selected `impersonationSessions.actorId` (real column `superAdminId`) and `emailChangeRequests.completedAt` (real column `consumedAt`) via `(schema as any).table` + `rows as any[]`. An undefined column reference makes drizzle's `orderSelectedFields` recurse into `Object.entries(undefined)` during the **synchronous** `_prepare()`, which throws *before* the `.catch(() => [])` is attached → uncaught 500. Fixed that file by importing the tables typed (drift now a compile error) + mapping to the real columns (also un-breaks the never-emitted "Email changed" event). **Remaining gap:** ~14 other sites still use `(schema as any).tableName` (e.g. [discovery.ts](hired.video/api/src/routes/discovery.ts), [featured-creators.ts](hired.video/frontend/../api/src/routes/featured-creators.ts), [job-analytics.ts](hired.video/api/src/routes/job-analytics.ts), [targeting-evaluator.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/advertising/targeting-evaluator.ts)) — each is a latent same-class 500 if a referenced column/table is renamed. Sweep them to typed imports (or a typed `requireTable` helper) so column drift fails `tsc`, not prod. |
| 1183 | T3 | 🔲 Open | Later | P3 | `feature` | **Manual DPO tooling for verified data-subject erasure requests — replaces the auto-purge worker user declined in #1149** (opened 2026-05-24 alongside #1149 — renumbered from #1168 — explicit user decision: "I don't think we should hard delete any user… delete shouldn't delete but rather anonymize the existing content and make private") | `deleteAccount()` in [account.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/user/account.ts) is now anonymize-on-delete: PII is scrubbed, videos go private, the row stays so historical activity (applications, transactions, audit trails) keeps a stable user_id. For verifiable GDPR Art. 17 / CCPA "Right to Delete" requests where the data subject specifically asks for ROW erasure (not just anonymization), we need a SuperAdmin-only tool that: (1) authenticates the request (subject identity verified — sign-in + email confirm, or DPO-attested manual review for unauthenticated requests routed via privacy@hired.video), (2) shows a preview of what would be hard-deleted vs cascade-nulled (jobs they posted, messages they sent, ledger entries, etc.), (3) executes the DELETE inside an explicit admin action with audit logging (admin_id + reason + before/after row counts). NOT a scheduled sweep — every hard-delete is a single human-attended operation. Until built, the DPO handles these requests manually via direct DB access + an audit-log entry. |

Unblocks: same-day percentile bands for any newly-launched assessment kind. |

| 536 | T4 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `quality` | **Email sync per-message LLM cost is unbounded — full-mailbox first sync on a heavy mailbox can fire hundreds of `email_classify` + `email_summary` calls in one cron tick** (2026-05-10) | [sync.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/email-integration/sync.ts) classifies every new message that wasn't caught by Stage-1 rules, then summarises every job-related message above 0.6. Heavy users (200+ unread inbox) on first connect could fire 100+ LLM calls in a single sync run — no per-user budget gate, no back-pressure, no batch dispatch. Today this is shielded by Stage-1 rules catching the obvious ATS senders, but if the user has lots of recruiter outreach below the rules' precision floor it can spike fast. Fix: (a) pass the user's `JwtPayload` into `runEmailSyncSweep` so per-user token-budget 429s land cleanly, (b) batch the summary stage (one LLM call summarising ≤10 messages at once via `callMessages`), (c) hard-cap per-sync-run classify count and roll the overflow into the next tick. Unblocks: predictable cost on full-mailbox-sync MVP. |
| 549 | T4 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `arch` | **Frontend ships with no `VITE_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` — backend's `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` is set, so every login was being hard-rejected with `400 TURNSTILE_FAIL`** (2026-05-12, reported via `/login?source=extension` flow from the Chrome extension) | The site key is referenced by [TurnstileWidget.tsx](hired.video/frontend/components/TurnstileWidget.tsx) and [use-turnstile.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/hooks/use-turnstile.ts) but does NOT appear in any `.env*` or `wrangler.toml` — Vite's build inlines it as `undefined`, the widget short-circuits via `if (!siteKey) return null`, and the form posts with no `cfTurnstileToken`. Backend was returning `TURNSTILE_FAIL` and locking everyone out (extension's "Continue in browser" tab + the in-popup email/password form both hit the same wall). **Mitigation in place (this commit):** [AuthService.handleLogin](hired.video/api/src/services/auth/AuthService.ts) now treats a captcha miss as a soft signal — records the bot_block_event and forces the email-OTP step-up after password verification (`captchaFailed → requiresMfa`). Real account holders prove humanness via inbox; bots without mailbox access stall at the second factor. Extension popup ([shared/login.js](hiredvideo-chrome-extension/shared/login.js) + [shared/login.html](hiredvideo-chrome-extension/shared/login.html)) hosts the OTP entry inline so users don't have to start over in a new tab. **Real fix still owed:** add `VITE_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` to the production frontend build (Cloudflare Pages env vars or `frontend/.env.production`) paired to the backend secret, so the silent challenge runs and only users who fail it get the OTP fallback. **Adjacent gap:** signup is still hard-blocked because [signupGuard.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/auth/signupGuard.ts) hasn't been given the same step-up rail (and adding email-OTP step-up to a NEW user creation flow is a bigger UX call). Unblocks: production sign-in for everyone, and re-enables silent bot defense once the site key ships. |

| 393 | T4 | 🔲 Open | Mid | P3 | `quality` | **No proactive monitoring for OAuth client-secret expiry** (2026-05-05) | Microsoft secrets expire (24-month max since the 2021 Azure default change), GitHub PATs/secrets can be rotated, LinkedIn/Google secrets don't expire by default but can be revoked. | 537 | T4 | 🔲 Open | Mid | P3 | `quality` | **Gmail sync fetches messages serially — bursts >50 messages will time out on a Cloudflare Workers sub-request budget** (2026-05-10) | [providers/gmail.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/email-integration/providers/gmail.ts) walks `users.messages.list` ids and calls `users.messages.get` one-at-a-time. ~300ms per call × 50 = 15s, ~200 messages = a minute, hits the 50-subrequest soft limit and the 30s CPU budget on Workers free tier. Outlook delta endpoint returns full message bodies in pages so it's fine. Fix: parallelise Gmail's `fetchMessage` loop with a small concurrency window (e.g. `Promise.allSettled` × 5), AND use `users.messages.batchGet` (not yet on the public REST API — would need the batch HTTP endpoint with a multipart body) when the count exceeds a threshold. Unblocks: reliable first-sync on heavy mailboxes. |

Gmail returns 429 with `Retry-After` + per-user quota guidance; Graph returns 429 with `Retry-After` + per-app throttling. Fix: shared `providerFetch` wrapper that honours `Retry-After` (single retry, capped delay so a worker invocation doesn't exceed the 30s CPU budget) and downgrades 429 to a `partial` sync rather than an `error`. Unblocks: graceful degradation on transient throttling. |

| 917 | T2 | 🔲 Open | Mid | P3 | `feature` | **No proactive token-health check — `invalid_grant` is only discovered when the user (or sync button) triggers an `/access-token` call** (opened 2026-05-21 — surfaced fixing the `invalid_grant` 502 toast) | The PWA-side sync at [sync-client.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/email-integration/sync-client.ts) only calls `/api/integrations/email/accounts/:id/access-token` when the user is on the Inbox tab and clicks Sync. A mailbox whose refresh token Google revoked overnight stays silently broken for days — the `status: "error"` flip in the route only fires on the next user-initiated sync. Worse, message backfill stalls without notification: users assume the lull is real-world inbox quiet, not a token-rotation failure. Fix: (a) a daily Worker cron `email-token-health` that calls `refreshAccessToken()` for every `status: "active"` row, marks the row `status: "error"` + `lastError` on `invalid_grant`, and skips the access-token result (we only need the refresh side-effect), (b) on first transition into `status: "error"`, enqueue a notification row ("Gmail (sean@…) needs reconnecting") so the user sees it in the same Inbox they're trying to use, (c) optionally email a transactional reminder if `error` persists > 72h. Unblocks: a user opens Outreach Monday morning to find a Reconnect prompt already waiting, instead of "why am I getting no emails?" Pairs with #915 (popover banner) — same surface lights up the notification. |
| 919 | T4 | 🔲 Open | Mid | P3 | `bug` | **No detection or migration path for `EXTERNAL_EMAIL_TOKEN_KEY` rotation — every existing refresh token becomes undecryptable garbage, the route then surfaces it as `invalid_grant` and floods every user with bogus Reconnect prompts** (opened 2026-05-21 — surfaced fixing `invalid_grant` toast) | [encryption.ts deriveKey](hired.video/api/src/services/email-integration/encryption.ts) derives an AES-GCM key from `EXTERNAL_EMAIL_TOKEN_KEY` (falls back to `JWT_SECRET` for dev). If that env rotates, `decryptToken()` either throws (caught) or decodes ciphertext to nonsense, the nonsense string gets POSTed as `refresh_token=...`, and Google returns `invalid_grant`. With #918's classifier in place, every mailbox would simultaneously transition to `status: "error"` and prompt users to reconnect — even though the tokens at Google's end are perfectly fine. Fix bundles: (a) version-tag the ciphertext blob (`v1:iv:ct`) so a key-rotation can ship `v1` + `v2` keys in parallel and decrypt either, (b) catch `decryptToken` failure specifically (vs. token-refresh failure) in [email-integration.ts /accounts/:id/access-token](hired.video/api/src/routes/email-integration.ts) and return `MAILBOX_DECRYPT_FAILED` (different code from `MAILBOX_REAUTH_REQUIRED`) so the UI can say "this isn't your fault — operator action needed" instead of asking the user to reconnect for nothing, (c) an admin migration script that decrypts-with-old-key + re-encrypts-with-new-key over the `external_email_accounts` table during rotation. Unblocks: safe key rotation + no user-facing mass-reconnect prompt when ops swaps the seed. |

| 1448 | T4 | 🔲 Open | Later | P3 | `perf` | **Drizzle-side projection for DTO-assembled list routes (#1445 phase 2)** (opened 2026-05-25 alongside #1445 partial). Every other list route besides `/api/notifications/` goes through a DTO assembler (`rowToCompanyMeta`, `toPublicCard`, `fetchPostDtos`, `toPublicUserCardDto`, `rowToTalentPoolEntry`) that reads the full DB row to compose downstream fields. To push projection through to the SELECT, each DTO needs to either: (a) accept a `fields` parameter and conditionally skip the columns the caller didn't request, OR (b) be split into "must-have base columns" + "optional columns by name" so the route can compose the SELECT shape dynamically. ~3 hours per endpoint. Most DTO assemblers do trivial mapping (`row.name → dto.name`) so option (a) is mostly mechanical. Worth it once a real Neon I/O budget complaint surfaces — until then, the Worker→client wire trim (#1444) is the dominant saving. |

(d) **Pen** — drawing trail behind a sprite (writes to an overlay canvas the runtime composites under sprites). (f) **Sound** — volume / tempo / instrument blocks (today only `play-sound` ships). (g) **Show-monitor** floating overlay for variables (Scratch's HUD widget for watching a var change live). Unblocks: full Snap!/Scratch parity for non-coder authoring. |

**Owned paths:** `frontend/components/{ui,layout}/**`, `frontend/lib/seo.ts`, `frontend/public/locales/**`, `shared/i18n.ts`

| 2235 | T4 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P3 | `quality` | **Card footer (`CardActions`) bottom-pin is not centralized — each card type re-implements (or forgets) the full-height flex-column layout, so action panels drift to mid-card in stretched grid rows** (opened 2026-06-21 fixing the /people grid) | [PeopleCard.tsx](hired.video/frontend/components/PeopleCard.tsx) was fixed by adding `flex flex-col h-full` to its `BaseCard` + `flex-1` to `CardContent` so `CardActions` pins to the bottom of the stretched grid cell. Other grid card types (ResumeCard, JobCard, CompanyCard, ServiceCard, etc.) rely on their own ad-hoc layout and can exhibit the same mid-card footer when content heights differ across a row. To close: fold the `flex flex-col h-full` shell + a `flex-1` content slot into [BaseCard.tsx](hired.video/frontend/components/ui/BaseCard.tsx)/[CardActions.tsx](hired.video/frontend/components/ui/CardActions.tsx) (e.g. a `pinFooter`/`fill` variant) so every grid card pins its footer by default instead of each card re-deriving the layout. | 2217 | T4 | 🔲 Open | Mid | P3 | `quality` | **i18n coverage detector over-counts TypeScript generics + proper nouns as "hardcoded", so a literal 100% is unreachable on high-coverage pages** (opened 2026-06-20 during the ≥80% completion sweep) | [scripts/i18n-coverage.cjs](hired.video/frontend/scripts/i18n-coverage.cjs)'s JSX-text heuristic `>([^<>{}]*)<` matches TS generic/arrow syntax (`useState<JobAlert>(null)`, `z.infer<typeof …>`, `React.ChangeEvent<…>`, ternaries `) : (`) as if it were visible copy, and its `isNoise` filter doesn't strip brand/proper nouns or contact emails (hired.video, info@hired.video, security@hired.video, SCORM, testimonial names). Net effect: the ~39 pages already at ≥80% that this sweep touched report 2–30 residual "hardcoded" flags that are **100% false positives** — verified via `--json` `samples` (e.g. Jobs.tsx h=24, Outreach.tsx h=21, PrivacyPolicy.tsx h=30 are all code/brand, not copy). To close: (a) strip generic/JSX-expression spans before the text-node scan (drop matches containing `=>`, `<typeof`, `) :`, or lowercase-only single-token identifiers); (b) add a brand/proper-noun + email allowlist to `isNoise`. Unblocks: the coverage % becomes a trustworthy completion signal (today it floors ~73% and cannot reach 100% even when every real string is localized). Until then, inspect `samples` before assuming a high-coverage page has a real leak. |

| 2249 | T4 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P3 | `i18n` | **Profession `/for/[audience]` hero label + blurb render English-only for es/fr-CA — the studio audience SSoT isn't localized** (found 2026-06-21 during the /for marketing-page rebuild #900) | The rebuilt [ProfessionLandingPage](hired.video/frontend/pages/landing/ProfessionLandingPage.tsx) localizes ALL chrome (benefits / how-it-works / FAQ / CTAs via the new `audienceLanding.*` keys in en/es/fr-CA), but the hero `<h1>` label + lede still render `audience.label` / `audience.blurb` straight from [audiences.ts](hired.video/frontend/components/studio/templates/audiences.ts) (the studio-template SSoT, English literals) — and those same English strings feed the page `<title>` + meta description + the CollectionPage JSON-LD `name`/`description`. So a Spanish/French visitor on /for/director sees localized sections but an English headline + SERP snippet. To close: add an `audienceProfessions.<id>.{label,blurb}` catalog (mirroring the life-stage `audiences.<key>.*` convention) + a small localize helper, resolved at render via `t()` with the registry English as `defaultValue`; keep audiences.ts the English SSoT. Pairs with the per-locale meta-tag gap #2116. Unblocks: fully-localized profession marketing pages + localized search snippets for the es-MX / fr-CA funnels. |

## Go-Live Readiness — CxO Gap Analysis

Executive view of what blocks production launch, organized by functional owner. Each finding cross-references the detail entries in the Consolidated Gap Register below. Last updated **2026-04-25**.

**Overall verdict:** The platform is **feature-rich and architecturally sound**, but launch readiness is gated by a tight cluster of legal, payout, and observability gaps that are individually small but collectively non-negotiable. The fastest path to live is a **scoped Day-1 launch** focused on the Pro / job-seeker / employer path, deferring the creator-monetization rails (articles, courses, partner program) until the payout pipeline is real.

### CEO — Strategic Readiness

**Strong**
- Positioning is coherent post-AI-CHRO redesign (#93). One product anchor ("your AI CHRO") that the rest of the surface area hangs off.
- Pricing is decisive: Free + Pro at $9.95/mo with itemized token packs ($3.99–$199.99). No Enterprise upsell to manage at launch.
- 95/5 rev-share standardised across every monetisation surface (#156). Creators see the same number everywhere.

**Risks**
- **Surface-area sprawl.** The roadmap covers jobs, recruiter CRM, ATS, video studio, voice marketplace, creator services, courses, articles, ad campaigns, partner program, outplacement, white-label, and B2B sales persona. No public commitment yet to a Day-1 wedge — "we ship everything" creates a positioning that doesn't fit a single buyer.
- **Bus factor.** Most P0/P1 surfaces are single-author. Recommend a written ownership map per surface before launch.
- **No public roadmap or changelog** for users; everything lives in this README.

### CFO — Revenue, Billing, and Payouts

**Strong**
- Payment provider abstraction with both **Stripe and Helcim** implementations (#47). Switchable via env var.
- Annual billing with 20% discount, 14-day trial, churn/cancellation/pause flow, AI usage metering, ad revenue tracking, seat-based company billing — all wired (#21–#67).
- Token-based AI metering replaces per-request rate limiting. Itemised token pack purchases live.

**Risks (go-live blockers)**
- **Partial payout pipeline.** ⚠️ Onboarding + ledger landed 2026-05-17 (#771): creators connect via Helcim ACH (tokenised bank, we never see routing/account) or Tremendous gift cards, accruals route through `PayoutLedgerService` (append-only double-entry; balance = SUM(amount_cents)), schema-drift fix dropped duplicate payout columns from `creator_profiles`, AES-GCM tax-ID encryption (#772) replaced the plaintext placeholder. **Still blocking:** #773 release worker (cron) that turns "balance ≥ $50 threshold" into actual `provider.createPayout()` calls — without it creators onboard and accrue but no money moves. Ship #773 before opening monetisation to a wider cohort.
- **No 1099 / W-9 / VAT compliance** in scope. W-9 / W-8BEN inputs land via PATCH `/me/payout-profile` (encrypted-at-rest as of #772) but year-end PDF / IRS-filing pipeline does not exist (#777 opened). US creators with >$600 lifetime settled payouts trigger 1099-NEC; international creators trigger W-8BEN; EU buyers trigger VAT MOSS. The reporting half is still unscoped.
- **Sales tax** on token packs and subscription is not visibly computed. Stripe Tax integration not detected.
- **#96 Enrichment credits** marketed as a Pro add-on but external provider is stubbed. Either ship behind a feature flag or hide the marketing.

### CTO — Engineering & Operations

**Strong**
- Drizzle schema is event-log first, polymorphic where it matters (reviews 0032, boosts 0034, feed subjects 0033). ~230 tables, 75+ migrations.
- BillingFacade + AccessPolicy split (#150) gives clean tier vs. audience semantics.
- OpenAPI 3.1 spec at `/api/openapi.json`, structured JSON logging, KV + in-process caching with explicit invalidation.
- TS strict across `api/`, `frontend/`, and `shared/`. Clean compile at the time of writing.
- Hono on Cloudflare Workers — predictable cold-start, predictable cost.

**Risks**
- **Test coverage gap (#1, still Open).** Only the BillingFacade/AccessPolicy module and a handful of tool handlers have unit tests (~264 passing). Most route handlers have **no automated coverage at all** — TS strict + manual smoke is the only safety net. **Block:** before launch, add at least smoke-level integration tests against `createApp()` for auth, billing, resume CRUD, job CRUD, and the creator opt-in flow.
- **No real-time transport.** `#41` (WebSocket chat) still Open. Recruiter↔candidate messaging works over REST but has no presence/typing/instant delivery. Acceptable for v1 if you set the expectation.
- **No background payout / reconciliation worker.** Cron exists for follow-ups, drip emails, and job-board scraping. Nothing yet for creator payouts (see CFO).
- **No documented runbook** for DB backup/restore, incident response, or secret rotation. Repo has the code; ops procedures are tribal.
- **Single-region.** Cloudflare Workers + Hyperdrive provides edge cache, but Postgres is single-region. Latency tail for APAC users is unmeasured.

### CPO — Product Completeness

**Strong**
- **Creator Program (post-2026-04-25 refactor #190)** is now coherent: per-program enrollment for Video / Voice / Services with proper event-log lifecycle, per-program legal terms, and a single management surface at `/services`.
- **Studio** has near-complete Wistia/Riverside parity per the capability matrix earlier in this README.
- **AI CHRO copilot** is the strongest single product surface and a clear anchor for the homepage CTA.
- **Marketplace + UnifiedSearch** integration (#191–#194) consolidated three previously-separate surfaces (Sell Hub, Creators browse, Search) into one navigation node.

**Risks**
- **Voice/Resume/Video template creator opt-in flows (#158, #159)** still Open. Voice clones already have a consent flow; templates do not. Creators can't yet monetise resume or video templates.
- **Courses (#160–#168)**, **Articles + Partner Program (#170–#175)**, and **Affiliate Network (#178)** are entirely greenfield. They should not be marketed at launch.
- **Pitch-deck reviewer feedback loop (#184–#186)** is the missing half of the agency v1 product. Don't market agency-tier without it.
- **Universal content rating widget (#181)** — backend done, no React widget. Listing cards have no social proof.
- **Outplacement (#100)** and **B2B Sales Persona (#104)** are P1/P2 but irrelevant to Day 1.

### CMO — Marketing Readiness

**Strong**
- Landing pages, Pricing, and Marketplace categories are **internally consistent** and accurate to current implementation (audited 2026-04-25 — see verdict in this PR's marketing audit).
- AI CHRO branding is coherent across `Index.tsx`, the chat panel header, and the system prompt.
- Marketplace category list matches `UnifiedSearch` filter pills exactly.

**Risks**
- **SEO content engine (#81)** is technically Done but light on editorial voice. Auto-generated resume/salary guides are SEO furniture, not authoritative content. No editorial cadence is defined.
- **Affiliate program (#74)** ships with tier math but creator-facing analytics are limited.
- **`isPlatformService=true` not yet flipped** on any seeded service. The "Enroll in Creator Program" funnel CTA on `/search?category=services` will not appear until SuperAdmin sets the flag on the curated offerings.
- **No public press / launch plan** in repo. Founder/marketing-lead handover gap.

### General Counsel — Legal & Compliance

**Strong**
- Centralised legal-document system (`legal_documents` + `legal_document_acceptances`) with versioning + IP/UA/context capture is **the right primitive** and already covers Voice Marketplace ToS §9a.
- Per-program creator terms scaffolded (`program_video_terms`, `program_voice_terms`, `program_services_terms`).

**Risks (go-live blockers)**
- **Placeholder terms text.** `0036_creator_programs.sql` seeds three program-terms documents whose body is literally `"Placeholder ... program terms. Update via the legal admin UI before going live."` **Block:** real lawyer-reviewed text must replace the placeholders before the wizard goes to creators.
- **§9b–§9f ToS additions still Open (#179).** Course Creator, Cert Issuance, SCORM Licensing, Writer Partner, Affiliate Network — all need text + acceptance flows. Not Day-1 if those products are deferred (see CPO).
- **Candidate share consent + GDPR audit (#186) Open.** Required for EU/UK/California operation if the agency redacted-share feature ships.
- **Content reporting / abuse path (#152) Open.** No user-facing way to report spam, impersonation, or inappropriate content. Required for any UGC platform under §230 / DSA.
- **Cookie consent + privacy preferences** exist (`privacyPreferences` table, cookie-preferences migration 0025) but the granular consent UX has not been audited against EU/UK requirements.

### CISO — Security & Trust

**Strong**
- Passkeys / WebAuthn (#65) implemented natively without external libs.
- OAuth providers wired for Google, LinkedIn, GitHub, Microsoft — secrets in Cloudflare env, no leaked credentials in repo.
- API keys hashed with SHA-256, key shown once.
- Password expiry (90d) + history (PBKDF2 last-5) + magic links.
- `ApiClient` Zod-validates every response (caught the `isCreator` strip bug in this session).

**Risks**
- **No external pen test** in repo. SOC 2 / SSO / data residency are listed as roadmap, not on Pricing.tsx — fine for SMB launch but blocks any enterprise deal.
- **Secret rotation procedure** not documented.
- **Webhook signature verification** is implemented per-provider (Stripe, Helcim, Dropbox Sign) but lacks a consolidated audit / replay-attack test.
- **Rate limiting** beyond AI metering: not visible. Auth endpoints, signup, magic-link request — none have visible per-IP rate limits.
- **Single-region DB** (see CTO) is also a security/availability concern for compliance frameworks that require geographic redundancy.

### Top 10 Hard Go-Live Blockers (cross-functional)

In priority order — these are the items that, if shipped open, expose the company to revenue loss, legal liability, or material UX failure on Day 1.

| # | Blocker | Owner | Gap ref |
|---|---------|-------|---------|
| 1 | Replace placeholder creator-program terms with lawyer-reviewed text | GC | #190, #179 |
| 2 | Ship the min-payout-threshold release worker (cron). Onboarding + ledger + Helcim ACH + Tremendous closed via #771 / #772; worker is the only thing still blocking funds movement. | CFO/CTO | #773 |
| 3 | Add 1099-NEC / W-8BEN / VAT collection for paying creators (W-9 / W-8BEN inputs encrypted-at-rest via #772; year-end reporting pipeline still missing) | CFO | #777 |
| 4 | Decide & flip `isPlatformService=true` on curated marketplace funnel offerings | CMO/CPO | #194 |
| 5 | Smoke-level integration test pack for auth/billing/resume/job/creator opt-in | CTO | #1 |
| 6 | Content abuse reporting path (`POST /api/reports`) | GC | #152 |
| 7 | Sales tax + VAT MOSS on token packs and subscription | CFO | (new gap) |
| 8 | Replace `#96` enrichment-credits stub OR feature-flag the marketing | CFO | #96 |
| 9 | Per-IP rate limiting on auth + magic-link + signup | CISO | (new gap) |
| 10 | DB backup/restore runbook + on-call escalation doc | CTO | (new gap) |

### Recommended Phased Launch

- **Phase 0 (now → 2 weeks)** — clear blockers 1, 4, 5, 6, 9, 10. Soft-launch to a beta cohort of paying Pro users only; creator monetisation initially limited to Articles (which need no payout rail until #172).
- **Phase 1 (2 → 8 weeks)** — Stripe Connect onboarding + min-payout-threshold worker (CFO blocker), Voice / Services creator monetisation flows, courses foundation (#160) on top of Studio reuse.
- **Phase 2 (8 → 16 weeks)** — SCORM 1.2 export (#161), Partner Program payouts (#172–#175), real-time chat (#41), enterprise/SSO if a customer pulls.

### LinkedIn-Comparable Scorecard

The CxO scorecard above measures absolute operational readiness. This second scorecard re-frames against the actual market comparable — **LinkedIn** — under the platform's positioning as "an HR-vertical marketing platform with native creator monetisation." Score = our capability ÷ LinkedIn's capability.

| Role | Score | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| **CHRO** | **70%** | Recruiter parity surprisingly close — Boolean search (#70), saved searches, AI sourcing agent (#66), talent pool, ATS connectors (Greenhouse/Lever/Workday + merge.dev planned), sponsored job listings, seat-based company billing all in. Gaps vs LinkedIn Recruiter: depth of network graph (1B profiles) and "talent insights" market-report tier. |
| **CPO** | **55%** | Profiles, feed, messaging, jobs, articles, videos, ads-manager hierarchy (#108–#111), AI CHRO copilot, polymorphic reviews/boosts/feed-subjects all present. Missing vs LinkedIn: Learning (#160 in motion), Events, Newsletters, Sales Navigator. **Where we exceed:** every content type carries native rev-share at 95/5; LinkedIn pays creators $0 by default. |
| **CRO** | **45%** | Self-serve Pro subscription + token packs + 20% annual + 14-day trial + churn flow + paid placements (#106–#108) + ATS integrations + white-label demo lead form + ROI calculator. Missing: Sales-Navigator-style intent-data tier, multi-tier Recruiter Lite/Pro/Corporate matrix, large-account contract motion. |
| **CISO** | **35%** | Passkeys/WebAuthn native, OAuth ×4 providers, API keys SHA-256, password expiry/history, JWT + magic links, Zod-validated responses, Cloudflare WAF/DDoS by default. Missing vs LinkedIn-grade: SOC 2 / ISO 27001 attestations, formal pen-test cycle, public bug bounty, security-incident runbook. |
| **CFO** | **35%** | Modern subscription billing (Stripe + Helcim), token-pack metering, ad revenue tracking, seat-based company billing, AccessPolicy/BillingFacade split. Missing vs LinkedIn (which inherits Microsoft's SAP-grade backbone): Stripe Connect for creator payouts, 1099-NEC / W-8BEN, sales tax + VAT MOSS, multi-currency pricing, revenue-recognition automation. |
| **CTO** | **25%** | Edge-native architecture (Cloudflare Workers + Hyperdrive Postgres + R2) is more modern than LinkedIn's monolith. TS strict, OpenAPI 3.1 public, structured logging, polymorphic schema patterns. Cannot match: 20 years of ML pipelines, real-time feed ranking, multi-region datastores, content-moderation ML stack, search infra at 1B-profile scale. |
| **CMO** | **25%** | Marketing pages internally consistent, AI CHRO branding coherent, SEO content engine + auto-guides shipped. Cannot match: brand recognition (zero vs LinkedIn's "Microsoft trust halo"), 1B-user social proof, B2B ad-network footprint. |
| **CEO** | **20% raw / 95% on wedge** | **Raw market position 20%** — LinkedIn has 20 years of network-effect moat + Microsoft distribution. **Strategic wedge 95%** — creator rev-share + AI CHRO + course/training-material authoring is genuinely defensible because LinkedIn cannot pay creators or pivot to AI-native authoring without cannibalising its own ad-funded model. The structural conflict is the moat. |
| | | |
| **Avg (capability)** | **~40%** | We're at ~40% of LinkedIn's surface area with **one axis** where we structurally exceed them. |
| **Wedge defensibility** | **95%** | LinkedIn cannot easily flip to paying creators without breaking its ad business, nor pivot to AI-native course authoring without cannibalising LinkedIn Learning's instructor-fee model. Two reinforcing structural conflicts. |

**Three things hired.video does that LinkedIn structurally won't copy:**
1. **95/5 creator rev-share** on every monetisable content type (articles, voice clones, video templates, services, courses). LinkedIn pays creators $0 by default.
2. **AI CHRO as the headline product surface** — UX built around the AI, not bolted on.
3. **Studio-as-authoring-tool** — the same engine that produces a 30-second job pitch produces a 10-minute training course (see Training Materials & SCORM section above). LinkedIn Learning still relies on instructor-uploaded video without AI-native authoring.

**Three things LinkedIn has that no amount of code closes the gap on:**
1. **Network effect** — 1B profiles of professional-graph data.
2. **Microsoft distribution** — Teams / Outlook / Office integration.
3. **Enterprise security posture** — SOC 2 / ISO 27001 / dedicated security org / public bug bounty. Achievable for us, but a 12–18-month investment.

**Practical implication for marketing:** every surface should lead with **"keep 95% of what you make"** as the headline — not feature parity claims. The wedge collapses if creators don't actually receive money, so the CFO blocker (Stripe Connect + payout pipeline) becomes the single most important capability to ship.
